# Listening stats

Listening stats count the plays on the card and show them in the Library: a hub with the time listened, the last 7 days, and shelves of tracks to play (Most played, New finds, Old favourites, Not played yet, Recently played), with a first row on Now Playing's navigation sheet. The player works them out from its own play log. It needs no network, writes nothing more to the card for a play than the play's own slot, and a tap never reads the card.

## 1. Status

**Status (2026-11-18): the fix's review applied (15 findings); host-gated, its device re-run owed.** The review of fix A + G (8cd3708) found, each fixed since: the caps' margin checked once, so a block another task frees above T between two caps was taken whole (now re-checked before each cap: Left); the arena's carve tried where no free block holds it, a false `[heap] FAILED` line (now asked of the largest free block first: NoBlock, no carve); the arena's fallback not carving as fe61db9 did (the first build lost 3 of 71 full logs fe61db9's hooks saved in the model; now the hold's caps are given back and the chunks carved as before, none lost); the take's `[listen]` line naming a fallback chunk as the ranges' block, printing `0 B carved` for a take that walked nothing, and overwriting the arena's NO PSRAM line; the walk's decisions untested on the host (now `autodj::WalkAbove`, the model walked through it); no case of a part-capped entry, a remount's overflow or a remount on a 40-row card, nor of a chunk failing with no arena; the docs' sizes and claims. The host: the new cases fail at 8cd3708 where they can run there (U9, U10, the fallback's case) and pass now; `pio test -e native` 111 suites, 2,380 test cases, all passed; the yardstick's variants pass, their figures unchanged; fixtures 671 up to date, `test_statsref` 64 OK, `test_playlog` 32 OK; core2 RAM 61,408 B (`.dram0.bss` 248 B over the move's, 56 B over the first build's: the take lines' state), IRAM left 5,157 B and S0's 25 objects. The device re-run is owed (5.14's "Its device re-run").

**Status (2026-11-17, later): the boot's hold split PSRAM (B1); fixed by A + G, host-gated, its device re-run owed.** B1's device runs (the move's c3f1f09 on the spare card, main's firmware as the control) found free PSRAM main's to the byte at every point, but the largest free block split for the session wherever the first frame or a queue came inside the boot's hold: 589,812 B against main's 638,964 B at the join (I-3m and I-3m2, three 16 KB classes; 40,960 B under after `qx`), 442,356 B after a full-log boot (I-1, twelve classes); the boots whose fold ended first had `D`'s free and largest main's to the byte (659,348/638,964 B), their join kept 4,992 B higher than main I-0's, whose join overlapped its own scan (not a stats effect). So the move failed 5.14's one-class acceptance there. The cause: the free block (T, about 58 KB) the spacers left above the hold's blocks at PSRAM's top, and the gaps between a full log's chunks, where those carves landed and pinned it. **Fix A** (5.14, "The hold's T"): caps close T after the spacers are given back (found by a heap walk, each checked against the address the walk gave), the spacers keep the margin with the block carved, the overflow is one arena (chunk by chunk when PSRAM has no block for it), and the hold is given back bottom-up; **G**: MARK's block by plain `psramAlloc`. The host: the hold's cases in the heap model (Y1-Y7), written first, fail at fe61db9 and are main's line with the fix; `pio test -e native` 111 suites, 2,376 test cases, all passed; the yardstick's variants pass; core2 at 9cc3825 RAM 61,352 B (`.dram0.bss` 192 B more), IRAM left 5,157 B and S0's 25 objects. **The device re-run is owed**: no figure of the fix's is measured yet (5.14 lists what it must show). **The recheck (2026-11-18)** found the hold short of a decoder's room: a track that follows one playing opens during a remount's overflow hold (never held), and with the caps held the session's first Opus found no room in the model from 48 KB less free than its session, where fe61db9's hooks left it some; the hold now gives its caps back, and the arena, when the largest free block wouldn't take the Opus's buffers (5.14, "A decoder's room"). **The final review (2026-11-18)** fixed when that is asked: the ranges' block at every take (no start is held while it alone is, and with PSRAM short its caps left a Play's first Opus no room where fe61db9's hooks left it some), the arena and its chunks only while a track plays (a start waiting at the arena's take opens nothing until the arena and its chunks are given back, so asking for it gave the caps or the arena back for nothing). It also found a take reported Closed when another task's block took T's rest's head between a cap and its walk (now Left) and a Lost take's line saying `0 B carved`. **Owner's calls open** (5.14, "What it costs"): the arena is the whole 16 chunks' room (262,080 B and its caps, about 400 KB for the hold) for any overflow, one chunk or 16, where a one-chunk overflow held about 18 KB before (an arena sized to what the scan can still need is the alternative); `Fold::held()` and `peak()` report the arena's bytes (the fold's `[listen]` line and `H`'s RAM line say 262,080 B for one chunk); G's re-carve of MARK's block if the device shows the two-class split the replay model predicts for 40 rows.

**The internal-RAM review** (2026-11-17, on f64c3f7; 5.14's "Moved to internal RAM", its last bullet): five findings, each fixed. MARK's block is back in PSRAM, at the top of its largest free block: on the internal heap its size, the card's, had no bound and no place in the budget, so internal RAM holds the object alone whatever the card. The budget is recounted with what G5 sees, the build's `.dram0.bss` over main's (2,096 B over S0's), not the object's 1,992 B: at the figures then held the steady floor would hold with 0-1 KB to spare, and the session's floor wasn't shown kept (measured since, both hold with about 6 KB to spare: below). The object in `.bss` never splits a free block but shrinks the DRAM heap above it, which can move a carve to the next internal heap. The console's looks that read the card (`H`'s card part, `Hd`, `Ht`) carved their work memory at once on the loop, where a decoder could carve its long-lived blocks beside them: they wait for `Hs`'s turn now. **S1's rework (2026-11-12 to 2026-11-16): designed, reviewed and built, R1-R7** (5.14). S1's device round 2 and main's control in the same script:
- S1's largest free block after an Opus update was 188,404 B against main's 294,900 B (under the mood plan's 245,764 B line where main is over it), and 57,344 B under main's from the boot. The comparison found the cause: the resident blocks, carved again by good fit at each take-back, move where the decoders' long-lived blocks land.
- The owner's decision, "Rework S1": one block of at most 3,584 B held from setup (the state, HEAD, MARK's first 16 rows, 96 records of the slots since the last save); everything else in `stats.bin` alone, merged at each save (the boot's, one each 64 plays, `Hs`, the rotation's); no give-back, no re-read after an update.
- The design's review (2026-11-12, integrity and memory) kept that core and found 17 things, each checked and folded in (5.14's list): a held tmp would have counted a save's records twice and a held compaction lost its delta; a full log's first play rotates, so an unsaved boot overflow lost up to 6,553 plays after one fault (now its save is tried four times, the boot's waits apart); statsref couldn't express three save moments; the boot's scan keeps its wait for an asked update and the update waits for the scan's blocks; the records' save runs at 96 whatever a fence; a section damaged under the session stops the saves; the model's decoder carves weren't the device's.
- With the device's decoder requests the multi-update model fails none of 360 steps over 72 histories for the rework (main's own queue 2,048 B longer: 2; S1 as built: 47), and none of D, round 2's own sequence; with the old shapes 2, both main's variation's. Re-run that way, the scan's 10,896 B work memory held across its passes cost 8 steps, so the scan holds only its 3,072 B of ranges; and a full log's overflow (about 270 KB, once a card) fails up to 29 steps when a tap on Play opens the Opus during it, wherever it sits, so a track's start now waits the 1.4 s or so it is held. The model doesn't reproduce round 2 on round 2's own sequence: the device re-run against main's figures, not the model, accepts the design.
- The build plan is 5.14's R1-R7 and a device re-run against main's figures, within the class of main's own figure. Since R7, 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11 and 13 describe the rework as built (until then they described S1 as built, 414488c). R1 is built: the yardstick is `test_autodj_join`'s `test_the_listening_stats_across_updates`, which failed on purpose against S1 as built until R6 made it the rework's (`pio test -e native` exited 1 on it meanwhile). R2 is built: `tools/statsref.py` keeps the rework's save moments (the records and their save, the fence, Held, a boot overflow's tries, behind) and its 34 sessions are the fixtures; until R5 runs them through the firmware, `test_stats_store` fails two cases on purpose too (5.14's R2 "As built"). R3 is built: `lib/core/StatsFold` is the rework's fold (the records, MARK and HEAD in a 2,656 B object, the boot's overflow in chunks, the cursors a save merges the card's rows with), and `test_stats_fold` checks its merges against statsref byte for byte; S1 as built's fold stayed as `statsfold::s1::Fold` (`lib/core/StatsFoldS1`) until R6 deleted it (5.14's R3 "As built"). R4 is built: `lib/core/StatsFile`'s store reads `stats.bin` in one pass (HEAD and MARK into the fold, the rest only checked as it streams, MARK's block carved at the boot for a card past 8 rows) and saves by streaming, nothing in RAM but the records: a counting pass and a writing pass over the card's sections merged with them, and a compaction over three streams between the two; `test_stats_file` holds its files to statsref's byte for byte on the index's 34 boots and on 32 of the 34 sessions run through the store alone; S1 as built's store stayed as `statsfile::s1::Store` (`lib/core/StatsFileS1`) until R6 deleted it (5.14's R4 "As built"). R5 is built: `lib/core/StatsGlue` and the firmware run the rework (a 3,200 B glue object, the device's, held from setup; the records' save at 64 at a pass the write gate lets through, before its slot, not behind a library update's fence until 96; a boot overflow's save tried four times, a track's start, Auto DJ's join and an asked update waiting while the scan's blocks are held, `LibraryUpdate::Wait::Stats`); the give-back is gone; `test_stats_store` runs every index boot and all 34 sessions through the firmware's glue byte for byte; S1 as built's fold and store stayed for `test_autodj_join`'s S1 run until R6 (5.14's R5 "As built"). R6 is built: `test_autodj_join`'s `StatsRun` runs the firmware's own glue on a card in memory, every block it carves through the model heap, and the yardstick passes: the rework fails none of its 65 steps nor of the wide set's 360 with the device's decoders (with the old shapes 2 of 360, both the control's own), its boot's join main's, and `test_the_listening_stats_in_a_model_heap`'s 13 shapes join the control's 360,436 B. It found that every boot's scan, not a full log's alone, holds one overflow chunk, so a track's start waits during every boot's scan (5.14's R6 "As built"; the rework review took the scan's look-ahead out, to the owner's default). S1 as built's fold, store and run are deleted. R7 is built: the docs (`docs/METADATA.md` 3.11, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, the README's `H`; this plan's 1, 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11, 12 and 13 rewritten to the rework: 5.14's R7 "As built"). The rework review is applied (5.14's build plan, "The rework review"). Its gate is met on the host (2026-11-16, at 39513f1: "The rework's gate", above). Nothing of the rework is flashed: the device re-run is next.

This document is the build plan, read and written at firmware bc29b54 (main; v0.14.0 released). PR #58 moves NEXT_RELEASE to 0.15.0 (`tools/version.py:54`), so the stats ship in 0.15.0 at the earliest.

**What S1 changed from this plan.** Each change is amended where its section stands, in the commit that made it, which says why:
- **The rework** (5.14, R1-R7, 2026-11-16; the owner's decision of 2026-11-12, "Rework S1", after S1's device round 2). Where the code departs from 5.14's design, each step's "As built" says so, and 5.14's text was fixed in the step's commit. The departures that matter: a save's work memory is 11,456 B on the device, not 10,896 B (R4: two containers, two row readers and the merge's rows beside the writer), 19,752 B with a save's two FatFiles; MARK's row is 28 B, keeping the seq a held tmp holds (R3); the glue's object is 3,200 B on the device (R5), under the 3,584 B `static_assert`; the compaction runs between the save's counting and writing passes (R4); the ranges' block is 1,284 B for 16 MARK rows and no ACKS row above them, 4 B over and aligned to 8 (R5); a boot overflow's save is tried again for no work memory and the card away too, not only a step the card failed (R5); the records sort by a heap sort (R5: `std::sort`'s introsort recursed on the loop's stack); a track's start waits through the headphones' `Hold`, and a track following one playing isn't held (R5), PlayGate deciding the rest since the rework review (`In::statsHold`); every boot's scan held one overflow chunk (its first piece reserved 128 slots, past the 96 records: R6), which the rework review took out (no look-ahead: `statsglue::kReserveAhead`), so a start waits only while a full log's fold holds chunks, once a card; and the model found the start's wait not load-bearing with the ranges' block at 1,284 B (R6) but load-bearing at its largest, 3,076 B (the rework review). statsref asks the records' save only at `play` and `hs` steps (R2). R7 folded 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11 and 13 into the rework; the give-back's text there is now history.
- **S1's device round 1** (2026-11-12 UTC, the owner's Core2; 9.5's "Round 1"). At the pass where the play log's write gate opened, the loop ran `Hs` before the Outbox wrote the slots that had waited for that same gate, so `Hs` saved "nothing new" with 4 plays waiting; and the update step waited for the gate only when the fold was already dirty, so a give-back found "nothing to save first" with 3 completed plays queued, and the ring took them. Now the plays waiting for their slots come first: `Hs` runs only at a pass with none due (`statsglue::turnOf()`), and the update step waits while one is queued, one behind a try the card failed too (`Glue::updateWaits()`; the fixes' review found it had waited only for those due, so a backlog behind a failed try could still fill the ring), so each is written, folded live, and held by that save (5.5, 6.7). The give-back's ring holds the Tracker's 32 plays, not 16 (5.6). `H` prints a save with nothing new against the card's generation, and `statsref.py` takes an ISO time ending in Z. The WriteGate, the update's safe point, the fence and the no-loss proof are unchanged.
- **The give-back** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-11, "Give back in updates"; gone with the rework, 5.14). S1's first gate found 6.7's pass rule failing in the heap model (C8, MODELLED): held through every library update, the stats' resident blocks came out of its largest free block at P = 19,478's resident ceiling and after a full never-synced log's fold (joined 180,212 B against the control's 360,436 B), and the ladder's steps 2-3 couldn't win back that much. Now, during a library update, the stats give their resident blocks back (MARK's kept), as Auto DJ's join already did, and read `stats.bin` from the card again after the update and the rejoin; a fold holding plays the card's `stats.bin` doesn't is saved first. Every shape of the model passes the rule (joined the control's 360,436 B; 16,384 B under it at P = 19,478 until S1's gate after the give-back, below): 6.7, and 5.4-5.8, 11, 13 where the data's rules change.
- **The give-back review** (2026-11-11, three lenses: no play lost, the heap model, the firmware's wiring), as built: a give-back whose save doesn't land (or a re-read that fails, a ring that overflows) can lose plays, to a rotation later in that session, where held through they'd have been saved; the docs, the comments and the `[listen]` texts had said "never a play" (5.5, 13; statsref's `update-behind-rotation`, 2 lost). A re-read the card fails is made again at later passes, the boot's waits apart, before the stats go off (5.4). The boot's scan waits while a library update is asked, and a fold not begun yet is given back, so it never begins or grows behind the update's memory check while the build runs (5.6, 6.7). The give-back's save waits for a pass the play log's write gate lets through (`LibraryUpdate::Wait::Writes`: the safe point is at least 20-30 s left, so it let a track's first seconds through) (5.5). `H` says what waits for the re-read from the glue's state (it had kept a count never reset). The heap model gains the device's room (16,368 B over the line after a FLAC update, MOOD-FILTERS Run D), which two P = 19,478 shapes crossed until S1's gate after the give-back (the chunks, next), and an update asked before the boot's fold (6.7).
- **The chunks** (S1's gate after the give-back, afbb05d, "Stats: what the gate found"; the rework keeps no RCNT or TRKD in RAM, and its overflow's chunks of 16,384 B hold 819 records, 4 B over). RCNT's and TRKD's chunks are 16,392 B, whole rows of both tables (1,366 and 683), not 16,384 B (1,365 and 682, 4 B and 16 B over), so RCNT's 4,096-row cap fills three and TRKD's 96 KB at the forced compaction six, as 11 has them; 16,384 B took a fourth and a seventh for the caps' last rows. Each carves the same 16,900 B (6.5). At P = 19,478 the re-read after an update is 178,576 B (194,880 B before), and joined is the control's in every shape of the model (6.7). The full log's fold peaks at 396,744 B (5.6, 11).
- **The S1 review** (2026-11-11) found, and the code now does: a fold out of memory is never saved, even with no work memory (it had trimmed plays never folded); a log file whose open finds damaged records is a file not read whole (5.6); the boot's fold's blocks and a remount's read of `stats.bin` wait for the scan's first piece, in a pass that may carve, never `setup()`'s or the card guard's (5.4, 5.6); stats off give every block back; RCNT is held to its cap at the fold's end, so a boot whose save doesn't land holds no full log's rows (5.6); a held tmp is promoted in its own call, off the save's stack (5.5, 11); a play waiting for the headphones, or a restart asked, counts as playing for a compaction (5.3); the heap model carves the glue and the read in the firmware's order and adds two shapes whose save doesn't land (6.7); the stack sums were too low (11); the gap counts the ACKS rows the root keeps (5.6).
- **The seq rule (C5) is its own commit**, f45fb48: the owner hasn't OK'd it and may drop it. Two of the proof's sessions (`nvs-back`, `log-deleted`) rest on it (9.5's C8 "As built").
- **The reference was wrong once** (c1b0751): after a lost base 1, a boot's compaction wrote under the name `stats.bin` names, and a cut there left the stats off for good. `tools/statsref.py`, the `base-other` fixture and 5.3 were fixed.
- **The fold's peak** on a full never-synced log is 396,744 B, through the journal and in `test_stats_fold`'s own scan (396,608 B and 412,992 B with 16,384 B chunks: the chunks, above), MEASURED-HOST: RCNT grows too, pruned only at the save. This plan had modelled TRKD's growth alone (5.6, 6.7, 11).
- **The fold** (5.6): HOUR and DAYS drop their oldest row at their caps; the boot's index is freed at the scan's end, before the save; with no memory for an index the fold goes on without it; live folds keep at most 64 rows unsorted.
- **The files** (5.3-5.5, METADATA 2.12.6): the base is read whole at the boot, not its header alone (11's card reads); damaged records are read once; `StatsCheck` keeps a tmp with damaged records (Unknown); a remount never renames `stats.bad`, which never shares a cluster chain; a held tmp is promoted only while its header is the one written; a base's frame is written by hand, last.
- **The glue** (9.5's C9 "As built"): its rules are portable (`lib/core/StatsGlue`), so the proof runs the firmware's own; the work memory is carved for each read and save, not held; the ending flag is set as `flush()` starts; Hs's refusals and their order; no PSRAM for the glue: no `Folds` (0.14.0's trim).
- **The proof** (9.5's C8 "As built"): a second set of statsref fixtures, `stats/sessions.json` (15 sessions, 154 steps); `FlakyFs` and `FaultFs` moved to `test/support/FaultFs.h`.
- **The play log's hooks** (C4): `openRead()` opens every log file, with or without folds (the same card commands); `Journal::Booted` gains `partial`; `written()` comes after `tried()`; the journal itself asks no save after a partial scan, and the glue refuses one too.
- METADATA 3.10's "not flashed" is corrected (1.1).

**S1's device round 1's fixes** (2026-11-11, local time; the code at e37104b, MEASURED-HOST; the tests from Git Bash, the builds from PowerShell with MSYSTEM unset; 9.5's "Round 1" has what the device showed):

The heap model: every joined, given-back, taken-again and held-through figure unchanged; only "free at least while placing" moved with the ring's 1,280 B (6.7).

- **The fixes' review** (2026-11-11, the code at f073406, MEASURED-HOST). The update step had waited only for a play due (`slotsDue()`), so while the Outbox's head waited out the 5 s after a try the card failed, the give-back went ahead with the backlog behind it (up to the Tracker's 32), which then went into the ring; one more play ending while given back passed it and turned the session's saves off (reproduced on the host: given back with 32 queued, ring 32, then 1 past it, behind). f073406 "Stats: what the round-1 fix review found": `PlayHistory::slotsQueued()` (the Tracker's plays, the journal ready, the card not away; no head's `due()`) is what `statsSaveWaits()` gives `Glue::updateWaits()`, so the update waits out a failed try, at most 10 s a play the card goes on failing (5.5); `Hs` keeps `slotsDue()`. `test_stats_store` 21: `test_a_backlog_behind_a_failed_try`, which failed before the fix (the give-back with plays queued behind a failed try). Round 1's L4 had the order at the gate too (9.5's "Round 1"). `TagConsole`'s scan line is 212 B at most with the play log's wait name (its comment said 208; the 224 B buffer holds it). `pio test -e native`: 111 suites, **2,352 test cases, all passed**, no ERRORED suite, 14 min 40 s (the builds ran beside it), exit 0. `card_fixtures.py --check` 663 up to date, `test_statsref` 59 OK, `test_playlog` 32 OK. `pio run -e core2 -e core2-dio` (the tree at f073406 with these docs uncommitted, `v0.14.0-52-gf073406-dirty`): both SUCCESS, every guard ok; core2 flash **4,474,431 B** (72 B over e37104b's), RAM **59,160 B** (unchanged); core2-dio 4,474,463 B; both link maps: **IRAM left 5,157 B**, the `libc_a-*` objects S0's 25.
- **9.5's Done-when: met on the host**, as at S1's gate (IRAM's 4 B still the owner's call). The device's runs owed: E, F, G, I, the rest of H, K and L, H and L again for these fixes, and main's controls.

**S1's gate: built and host-gated with the give-back; device pass owed.** 9.5's Done-when commands again after the give-back and its review, 2026-11-11, at afbb05d (MEASURED-HOST; the tests from Git Bash, the builds from PowerShell with MSYSTEM unset). At dc77a7e the heap-model case still failed as the review had left it: `test_autodj_join` ERRORED on the device's room alone (P = 19,478 with 300 slots, and with both saves refused: joined -16,384 B); that run was stopped once it had shown this, 60 suites in, every other one passed. The gate found the cause, which was not the ladder's to answer: a chunk of 16,384 B holds no whole number of RCNT's 12 B rows (1,365, 4 B over) or TRKD's 24 B rows (682, 16 B over), so RCNT at its 4,096-row cap took a fourth chunk for its last row, and TRKD at the forced compaction's 96 KB a seventh, where 11's budget has three and six. The model's P = 19,478 shapes load RCNT's cap, and that fourth chunk is the one the re-read took from the largest free block. Fixed in afbb05d ("Stats: what the gate found"): chunks of 16,392 B, 1,366 and 683 rows, nothing over, which carve the same 16,900 B on the device's heap (6.5); the re-read at P = 19,478 is 178,576 B (194,880 B before) and every shape's joined is the control's (6.7). No test was weakened: `test_stats_fold`'s memory case asserts the caps' chunks (3 and 6) and the boot whose save doesn't land RCNT in exactly 3; the heap model loads TRKD's whole 96 KB at P = 19,478 (4,096 rows, not the 4,092 six 16,384 B chunks held) and pins every shape again, the device's room check unchanged. With the fix undone (16,384 B), `test_stats_fold`'s memory case and the heap-model case fail.

**The give-back review's fixes** (2026-11-11; MEASURED-HOST, as above; "What S1 changed" has the findings):
- `pio test -e native`: 111 suites, 2,349 Unity cases: 2,348 passed, 1 failed (pio counts 2,350: the failing program's exit as one more; 13 min 3 s). The failure is `test_autodj_join`'s heap-model case, on purpose: the device's room alone (6.7), for P = 19,478 with 300 slots and with both saves refused (joined -16,384 B); every other assertion of it passes, and every figure pinned before is unchanged. New: `test_library_update` 38 to 39 (the update waiting for the write gate); `test_stats_store` 18 cases, its give-back case grown (the re-read the card fails once and at every read, a newer file, an update before the boot's fold, with a remount's read to make, and with every re-read failing) and its sessions 24; the heap model's two shapes of an update before the boot's fold (both pass). Every other suite passed.
- `python tools/card_fixtures.py --check`: 647 files up to date (634: `update-behind-rotation`). `test_statsref`: 58 OK. `test_playlog`: 32 OK. `test_synthtransfer`: 63 OK.
- Mutation checks: the glue's changes 8 of 10 caught (the two missed equivalent: behind with the boot's fold to come needs another `stats.bin` without a remount; the reads' count reset at a scan begun again is reset on the rescan path too); the update step's wait for the write gate 1 of 1.
- `pio run -e core2 -e core2-dio`: both SUCCESS, no warnings, every guard ok; RAM 59,160 B as before (a count gone, one added); IRAM left 5,157 B and the 25 `libc_a-*` objects S0's (the link map, both builds). core2's flash 4,473,919 B from the tree before its commits (`v0.14.0-39-g43f98fb-dirty`, its string 6 B longer than a clean one's), 1,752 B over the give-back's 4,472,167 B; core2-dio 4,473,951 B. The lines' frames, read from the core2 ELF, in 11.
- **9.5's Done-when: not met** (the heap-model case, the owner's call; IRAM's 4 B as before). Not checked on a device. (The heap-model case is met since: "S1's gate", above.)

**Designed** on the Claude Design canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/MhGiBt7yLecJZEErFQgSTq. Its final frames are Entry, Main, MostPlayed, NewFinds, NoClock, EarlyDays, NoPlays, NowPlayingSheet and Week. AlbumPlays was dropped by the owner (decision O3). The A- (Glance), B- (Recap) and C- frames are the directions not taken (14). The final spec lived in a scratch folder that Windows cleans up after about a week, so its essentials are inlined here (sections 2, 4, 5, 7, 8). Where this plan departs from it, 1.1 and 3.2 say so. The nine final frames are in [docs/img/listening-stats](img/listening-stats/README.md), captured from the canvas (C1).

**Picked by the owner, 2026-11-10:** O1-O4 and O6 of 3.1. **O5 is open.**

**Assumed:** everything else. 3.2 lists each default with its alternative, and the owner can overrule any of them.

Line numbers are at these commits:
- firmware bc29b54
- phone app (`mstream_music`) d17c3fa5
- terminal player origin/main 410444f, and `claude/card-transfer` 3edd4a4 (PR #56)
- `docs/METADATA.md` as S0's branch holds it: main 241642d's, with C2's edits and S0's review (main since bc29b54 changed only `tools/version.py` and docs, so the firmware's lines hold at 241642d too)

S1's commits have since moved lines: `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp`'s and `src/app/PlayHistory.cpp`'s, and `docs/METADATA.md`'s (its status paragraph, 2.12.6, 2.18, 3.10 and the new 3.11 add lines). The references here stay at the commits above.

Paths with no repository named are the firmware's.

Labels:
- **READ:** from the code or docs.
- **MEASURED:** on the device, quoted from the docs.
- **MEASURED-HOST:** a script or test run on a PC.
- **ESTIMATED:** the docs' own word, or a scaling of their figures.
- **MODELLED:** arithmetic, not run.
- **GUESS:** judgement.

### 1.1 Where the spec and the design run's maps disagree with the code

The code wins. Each item is READ at bc29b54.

- **The spec's "the card worker saves stats before `finishScan()` trims" has no window to run in.** `finishScan()` runs on the loop, called from the scan's last piece (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:315`), and in one call it stores the boot count, trims and sets Ready (`:318-347`). The save runs there, on the loop (5.5).
- **A journal state between the scan and Ready would drop plays.** `append()` returns Off for any state but Ready (`:523`), and the Outbox then drops that play for good (`:724-729`). `Outbox::flush()` would spin to its deadline (`:738-744`), since `scan()` returns true for any state but Scanning (`:246`). So no new state.
- **The scan reads nothing on a new epoch with no ACKS** (`:162-164`). After an NVS erase the old epoch's slots, and other players' (O2), would never be folded. With stats on, the scan always reads (5.6).
- **The scan reads the newest file first:** plays.jnl, then plays.1.jnl, then plays.2-9 (`:81-86`, `:249-256`). Fold rules that depend on order take it from the slot's own fields, never from the scan's order (5.6).
- **The spec's three files renamed one by one** (stats.bin, stats-trks.bin, stats-trks.d.bin) can disagree after a cut between two renames: rows counted twice or lost. This plan has one commit file that names its base file (5.2, 5.3).
- **"Skip the trim when the save fails"** (spec 8.3.3) can deadlock a full card: no save because it is full, no trim because the save failed. This plan trims a file when the MARK already on the card covers it (5.5).
- **The rotation deletes `plays.1.jnl` mid-session** with no ACKS check (`:411-463`, the delete at `:437`). The spec doesn't mention it. On a card never synced it is the only deletion. This plan saves first when that file holds a play the card's MARK doesn't cover (5.5).
- **A resident table keyed by track id can't name the rankers' hashes.** RCNT and TRKS are keyed by path hash, so the 7- and 30-day tops, New finds and Recently played come out as hashes. This plan keeps no per-track table resident (6.1).
- **The spec's walk cost** ("1.5 s idle, 3 s playing" with Auto DJ off) is the whole join's. A walk for stats is Folders and Hash only: no card reads, no sort. ESTIMATED 0.1-0.2 s of CPU (6.3).
- **The spec's redraw costs are low.** A row redrawn in place is about 11 ms with its push (`src/ui/ListView.h:273-274`), not 3 ms; a header push (320 x 36) about 5 ms, not 1. Section 7.10 uses these.
- **METADATA 3.10 says the play log was "not flashed"** (`docs/METADATA.md:7233`). The 0.14.0 checks ran it on the device: slots written in 14.8-16.6 ms (`docs/RELEASE-0.14.0-CHECKS.md:60`). S1's docs commit (C10) corrected it.
- **The terminal's time zone on Windows.** The design run's process map said `src/admin/tz.rs` gives no zone on Windows. Terminal origin/main reads the zone Windows is set to since 5eac90c (2026-10-29; terminal `src/admin/tz.rs:1-10, 32-49`). #56's branch (3edd4a4) doesn't have 5eac90c: there a Windows machine gets no zone (its `src/admin/tz.rs:6-7`). No branch sends it in `@time` yet (10).
- **ScanScheduler's jobs** are at `lib/core/ScanScheduler.h:101-102` (`kJobs` 8), not `:119-120`.
- **The log prefix.** `[stats]` is the audio's line (console `s`). The stats' lines are `[listen]`.

## 2. What the listener gets

**The way in.** Library > Artists gains a first row: seven small bars for the last seven days, "Listening stats", and "7 h 37 min in the last 7 days". It scrolls with the list. A tap opens the hub. From Albums or Folders: a tap on Artists brings its list back, a second scrolls it to the top, where the row is. Now Playing's navigation sheet (a tap on the cover or the title) gains a first row, "Go to Most played" with "9 plays · last Sun · 1 skip" under it. It opens the shelf at that track, tinted.

**The hub**, Listening stats. Under its title, the time listened since counting began ("74 h 10 min since 1 Sep"). Six rows follow, each with an icon, a title and a line:
- Last 7 days: "7 h 37 min · 118 plays"
- Most played: the top three titles
- New finds: "41 tracks first heard in 30 days"
- Old favourites: "38 tracks, played often, not lately"
- Not played yet: "612 albums since 1 Sep"
- Recently played: "Slow Lantern · 25 min ago"

**Last 7 days** is a page of its own: the time listened and the plays, seven bars with today in violet, the day listened most ("Saturday was your big day: 2 h 1 min"), and how often Auto DJ's picks became plays.

**The shelves** are Library lists that play like any list.
- Most played opens on All time, with a tray for 7 days and 30 days (O1). Each row: its rank, the title, "Artist · 12 plays".
- New finds: tracks first heard in the last 30 days, and who found them ("found by Auto DJ").
- Old favourites: tracks with 5 plays or more and none in 30 days.
- Not played yet: albums with no play, A-Z, with covers.
- Recently played: tracks newest first, under day headings.

A tap on a track opens its bar (Play, Play next, + Queue); a hold opens its sheet, as on an album page. The top bar plays the shelf.

**Early and odd states.**
- Nothing played yet: the hub is an empty state with Shuffle all.
- The clock never set: the totals and Most played (All time) work; the rest that needs dates says so, with the fix ("Plug in to the MP3 Player tab").
- The first month: New finds, Old favourites and Not played yet say when they open ("Opens 6 Dec, after a month of plays"), and Most played's tray grows with the days counted.
- No card: the row goes, and an open hub says plays can't be counted.

**What doesn't change.**
- A, B and C stay prev / vol-, play-pause / output, next / vol+.
- Album and artist pages stay as they are (O3).
- The play log's slots, files and ACKS rules stay as METADATA 2.20 has them, but for these device-side changes (5.5, 5.6): the boot's scan reads on a new epoch; a log file is trimmed only once the stats hold its plays; a rotation may save the stats before its delete; the boot's seq rule also takes the stats' and ACKS's highest seq for this device and epoch.

**The same as mStream:** a play is `playedMs >= min(30 s, half the track)` (mStream decides by this rule: `docs/METADATA.md` 2.20.6, "The post"); its id stays the slot's (2.20.4).

**Where it differs:**

| Topic | mStream | The player |
|---|---|---|
| Whose plays | The account's, from every client | The plays on this card, from every player that wrote them (O2) |
| Which plays | Those posted | The slots on the card. Plays under 1 s were never logged (`lib/core/PlayLog.h:377`) |
| Days | Its own time zone | UTC days until the terminal's zone arrives (O4) |
| History | Retention, 24 months by default | Since counting began on this card |
| A renamed or converted file | The server's track | A new path hash: its old plays stay in the totals and leave the shelves (6.6) |

## 3. Decisions

### 3.1 The owner's (2026-11-10)

| Id | Decision | Status | Alternative |
|---|---|---|---|
| O1 | Most played opens on All time. Its tray still offers 7 days and 30 days | **owner's pick** | Open on 30 days (the spec) |
| O2 | Every player's plays on this card count: no deviceId filter | **owner's pick** | This player's only |
| O3 | Album pages stay as they are: no plays line on album track rows. Per-track plays show in the shelves and the Now Playing sheet's first row only | **owner's pick** | The plays line (frame AlbumPlays) |
| O4 | Build on UTC days now. The terminal's time zone in `@time` comes later, applied at display only | **owner's pick** | Wait for the zone |
| O5 | When Not played yet opens | **OPEN.** Assumed: after 30 days of counting; sooner once mStream's counts reach the card (later, not v1: 5.11, 14). With the clock unset (counting days unknown): disabled, as New finds (4.4) | From the first day |
| O6 | The console's silent test mode `z` keeps logging plays, and they count | **owner's pick** | Not folded |

O5 is one constant in one function, `statsview::notPlayedOpen(countingDays, dated)` (7.11), so the answer changes one line and its test. Its no-clock case goes to the owner with it.

### 3.2 Smaller defaults made by this plan

All are the plan's, and the owner can overrule each.

| Area | Default | Alternative |
|---|---|---|
| Data | One commit file, `stats.bin`, that names a base file of per-track rows (5.2, 5.3) | The spec's three files |
| Data | The save runs synchronously on the loop at the scan's end, before the trim (5.5) | A worker save, with a journal state between scan and Ready (1.1 says why not) |
| Data | A file is trimmed only when the card's MARK covers every play in it. A failed save still trims what the old MARK covers | Skip the trim when the save fails |
| Data | A rotation over a `plays.1.jnl` holding plays the card's MARK doesn't cover saves first, in the orderly end's flush too when 1 s of its budget is left. A failed save there doesn't hold the rotation back | Let those plays go uncounted |
| Data | The boot's seq rule takes the highest of NVS's seq, the card log's, MARK's and ACKS's for this device and epoch (5.6). 2.20.4 allows it: a device MAY reserve seqs | MARK only; or today's rule |
| Data | Drop notes are not folded. No "lost" figure in v1 | Fold their counts into a lost figure |
| Data | A slot with an unknown outcome reads as stopped; source 0 and above 3 count as "you" | |
| Data | HOUR keeps its newest 840 rows and DAYS its newest 400, never pruned by date; RCNT keeps 32 days counted back from the earlier of today and the newest dated day folded, at most 4,096 rows, and nothing by date with the clock unset (5.2) | The spec's 13 months of HOURS; pruning by date from today |
| Data | No listened time per track | 4 B more a row |
| Data | `lastOrd` is a fold round (one a boot fold, one a session), not a counter a play | A counter a play: 4 B more a row |
| Data | firstAt is a track's earliest dated play, whatever the order of its plays; a track with no dated play is never a New find (5.2) | The first play's time, dated or not |
| Data | MARK rows are kept for good: one a (deviceId, epoch) ever folded on the card (5.2) | ACKS's leave-out rule |
| Data | A boot whose scan couldn't open or read a log file whole: no save this session; the next boot folds again (5.6) | Save what was read |
| Data | A save is refused when the card's `stats.bin` isn't the copy RAM stands on (another player saved meanwhile, or it was deleted on a PC) (5.5) | Save over it |
| Data | A `stats.bin` unread at the boot: no fold, no save, no trim this session; read again (once, no waits) at a remount before the boot's first slot | Fold into a session-only view |
| Data | A corrupt `stats.bin` is renamed `stats.bad` and counting starts again from the log (the spec's rule) | Keep it and stop |
| Data | The base is compacted only at a boot's save while nothing plays, when the delta is over 32 KB or a quarter of the base; past 96 KB at a boot's save at the update step's safe point, else at the next boot's (5.3) | Any save |
| Data | A newer minor of `stats.bin` is read; a save over it drops what it added, so a minor adds only what a save can do without (5.12) | A newer minor holds the saves back |
| Map | The walk runs alone, through code shared with Auto DJ's join, never in the join's pass (6.2) | Ride the join while Auto DJ is on |
| Map | No resident per-track table: ids only for RCNT's hashes and the lists' | The spec's played-track table |
| UI | The hub's Most played line shows the All time top three, the shelf's opening window | The 30-day top three (spec 3.2) |
| UI | Most played's All time sub is "since 1 Sep" (rule 5.4 of the spec: every lifetime figure says its window) | "all time" (frame MostPlayed) |
| UI | A window with no plays keeps its tray and shows "Nothing in this period." under it | Fall back to All time |
| UI | The top bar's Play follows the house's container rule: with shuffle on it starts at random (`src/ui/LibraryPage.cpp:810-815`) | Always from No. 1 (spec 3.3) |
| UI | The NP sheet's row shows only when the target shelf holds the track: the All time top 50, else Recently played's 200 | Open Recently played at its top |
| UI | The NP sheet's row waits for the track's record: no row until it is read | The row without its skips |
| UI | Recently played's lines give relative times within 24 h, then the artist alone; no clock times while days are UTC | "· 21:40" (spec 3.4) |
| UI | Ranks on Most played, New finds and Old favourites; none on Recently played | |
| UI | "(UTC)" only in Week's sub | On every date |
| UI | The tray splits at each gap's middle plus `filtergrid::skew()` (7.9) | The spec's 114 / 220 |
| UI | The artist page's " · N plays" is left out, read as part of O3 | Add it: about 5 KB of sums |
| UI | Stats unread, newer or with no memory: no entry row, as with the log off | A line saying so |
| UI | Totals show once the boot's fold ends; names once the walk ends (4.4) | Totals from the card at once |
| Frames | "No plays yet" (the phone app's `listeningEmptyTitle`) for the frame's "Nothing played yet"; "Listening time" (`listeningTileTime`) for "Listened"; "Recently played" (`autoDjRecentlyPlayed`). No translation owed for them but the short forms in 8.1 | The frames' words, translated |
| Frames | NoClock's open Not played yet row is drawn disabled, with `stats.needs_dates` (O5's no-clock case, 4.4) | Open, as the frame draws it |
| Frames | Toasts follow the code: two lines only when the text is too wide for one Body line (`src/ui/Overlays.cpp:105`). "Needs a month of plays: Opens on 6 Dec" is one line in English | A forced split |
| Frames | The frames are browser renders (`.dc.html`): checked for layout and colour, not pixel for pixel | |
| Console | `H` for the stats (history), `uiS…` for the screens (9.3) | |
| Time zone | `@time`'s field 1, minutes east of UTC, kept in NVS `clock/tz` at schema 7 | In `stats.bin`'s HEAD |
| Release | S1 may ship on its own: counting starts with it. S5a runs before any build carrying S1 boots the owner's music card (9.9) | Hold until S3 |

## 4. The behaviour, precisely

### 4.1 Definitions

| Term | Rule |
|---|---|
| A slot that counts | Kind 1, valid (MPPL, major 1, its CRC), used (epoch not 0, seq at least 1, deviceId not all zeros: `docs/METADATA.md` 2.20.6 item 2), once per (deviceId, epoch, seq) |
| The line | `min(30,000 ms, durationMs / 2)`; 30,000 ms when durationMs is 0. The half is not rounded, as mStream's `decideCounted` takes it (playedMs ≥ 30,000, or playedMs ≥ durationMs × 0.5): in integers, a play is 2 × playedMs ≥ min(60,000, durationMs) |
| A play | A slot that counts with playedMs at or over the line, whatever its outcome |
| A skip | Outcome 2 (skipped) with playedMs under the line. Completed or stopped under the line is neither. Outcome 0 or above 3 reads as stopped (2.20.3) |
| Listened time | The sum of playedMs over every slot that counts: plays, skips and the rest |
| Source | 3 Auto DJ, 2 shuffle, anything else "you" (1 manual, 0 not said, above 3) |
| Dated | startedAt from 2026-01-01 to 2099-12-31 UTC (`wallclock::believable()`, `lib/core/WallClock.h:27-31`). Else undated: 0 while the clock was unset, or another device's clock |
| Its hour, its day | Of startedAt, UTC. hour = (startedAt − kMinMs) / 3,600,000; day = (startedAt − kMinMs) / 86,400,000; day 0 is 2026-01-01 |
| A time in a row | Seconds since 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z, plus 1. 0 means none (lastAt) or only undated plays (firstAt) |
| Today | The board's clock now, UTC. None while it is unset (`app/BoardClock`) |
| Counting since (sinceDay) | Set once, at a fresh `stats.bin`'s first save: the day of the earliest dated play folded, but not after today when today is known; else today; else the root's commitTime's day (`lib/core/CardManifest.h:68`, Unix seconds UTC: `docs/METADATA.md:843`), flagged; else unset until a save that knows a date |
| Counting days | today − sinceDay + 1, at least 1 |
| Windows | 7 days: today and the 6 days before. 30 days: today and the 29 before. All time |
| Times | `format.hours_minutes`, unpadded minutes: "7 h 37 min". Under an hour, `format.minutes`: "45 min" |

### 4.2 What each number is

| Where | Number | From |
|---|---|---|
| Entry line, hub row 1, Week's readouts | Time listened and plays in the 7-day window | HOUR, summed over the window's UTC days |
| Entry and hub bars (24 px), Week bars (44 px) | Each day's time listened; the top day is full height | HOUR by UTC day. No bar for a day before sinceDay, or with nothing |
| Hub header sub | Lifetime time listened, since sinceDay | HEAD |
| Hub, Most played's line | The All time top three's titles, cut with "…" like any names | The All time list |
| Hub, New finds' line | Tracks whose earliest dated play is in the 30-day window | TRKS |
| Hub, Old favourites' line | Tracks with 5 plays or more and none in the 30-day window | TRKS |
| Hub, Not played yet's line | Albums with no track played, since sinceDay | The album bits (6.1) |
| Hub, Recently played's line | The newest track's title · how long ago | The Recently played list |
| A shelf row | Its count in the shelf's window | RCNT (7 and 30 days) or TRKS (All time) |
| Week line 1 | The top day of the 7 by time listened; a tie goes to the later day | HOUR |
| Week line 2 | Auto DJ's plays / (its plays + skips) over the 7 days, rounded to a percent; shown only when the sum is 5 or more | DAYS |
| NP sheet detail | The track's plays, its last day, its skips (omitted at 0) | Its TRKS row (base ⊕ delta) |

### 4.3 The shelves

| Shelf | Rule | Order, ties | Cap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Most played | Plays in the window, at least 1 | Plays, most first; then the latest play (RCNT's day, or lastAt), newest first; then hash | 50 |
| New finds | firstAt dated and inside the 30-day window | Plays; then firstAt, newest first; then hash | 50 |
| Old favourites | Plays at least 5; lastAt dated and before the 30-day window | Plays; then lastAt, newest first; then hash | 200 |
| Not played yet | Albums of `albumsAZ()` with no track played | A-Z (`lib/core/LibraryIndex.h:399-400`) | All |
| Recently played | Plays at least 1 | lastAt, newest first; then rows with no dated play, by lastOrd, newest first; then hash | 200 |

- "Then hash": path hashes as unsigned 64-bit integers, the smaller first.
- A hash that names no track of this library (an orphan: 6.6) is left out of a shelf. The rankers keep 16 rows past each cap, so a few orphans don't shorten it.
- Recently played's headings are the UTC day of lastAt: "Today", "Yesterday", then "Sun 8 Nov" (`format.weekday_date`, 8.2). With no clock: "This session" (tracks played since the boot), then "Earlier".
- A track only skipped has no row on any shelf. Its album still counts as not played.

### 4.4 States

| State | Trigger | Entry row | Hub | Shelves, Week |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Off | The log off (no card records, no PSRAM, NVS refusing: `src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:99-123`); `stats.bin` or its base unread after the retries (5.4); a newer major; no memory for the fold | Absent | Not reachable | — |
| No card | `cardAway()` or the card held (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:91-93`, `:185`) | Absent from the next `enter()` | The one line "No card: plays can't be counted" | The same line |
| Folding | Stats read, the boot's fold under way | "Counting plays…", no bars | No sub; every line "Counting plays…" | "Counting plays…" |
| Naming | The fold done; the lists or the walk not yet | Line and bars | Header, Last 7 days; the other lines "Counting plays…" | Week works; the shelves "Counting plays…" |
| Ready | | Line and bars | Everything | Everything |
| Empty | No counted play (HEAD's plays 0, none this session) | "Plays show up as you listen", no bars | The empty state (7.5) | — |
| No dates | The board's clock unset | "6 h 10 min since 5 Nov", no bars | Row 1 a status row (7.4); New finds and Old favourites disabled; Not played yet disabled with `stats.needs_dates` (counting days unknown; assumed with O5); Most played without its tray | Week not reachable; Recently played by session |
| First month | Counting days under 30 | As Ready | New finds, Old favourites and Not played yet (O5) disabled: "Opens 6 Dec, after a month of plays" | The tray by age (7.6) |
| Updating | Behind the update's fence (`Library::index()` null) | Absent: `topBar()` is false with no index (`src/ui/LibraryPage.cpp:585`) | Not reachable | Not reachable. The fence returns the Library tab to its root (`src/ui/Ui.cpp:748-755`) and Live does again (`:737`); the snapshots are dropped there. Now Playing's jump refuses with no index (`src/ui/NowPlayingPage.cpp:1140-1143`) |
| Synthetic library | `uil<n>` (`src/ui/LibraryPage.cpp:74`) | Absent | — | — |

- Whether the entry row is there is decided at `enter()`: adding or removing item 0 while Artists shows would move every artist by 42 px.
- A remount doesn't reset the Library tab (`src/main.cpp:3237-3284`); only a rebuild (`:1071`) and an update's Live (`:3467`) do, through `Ui::libraryChanged()` (`src/ui/Ui.cpp:731-746`). An open hub, shelf or Week follows the card state itself (the No card line), at its next `update()`.
- The status band (a scan, tags, an update) shows on the hub and the shelves as on any Library list. It never shows on Week, a page of its own.

### 4.5 Toasts

| Toast | Kind | When |
|---|---|---|
| "Needs dates: Plug in to the MP3 Player tab" | `Ui::warn` (amber, 1.8 s) | A tap on New finds or Old favourites, disabled for want of dates |
| "Needs a month of plays: Opens on 6 Dec" | `Ui::warn` | A tap on a row disabled in the first month |
| The existing `act()` toasts, with Undo and View | toast | Play next, + Queue on a shelf |
| `toast.shuffling` | toast | Shuffle all, the empty state's |
| `toast.no_memory` | toast | A shelf's snapshot can't be carved |

A disabled row's tap also gives the inert double buzz (`Input::missBuzz()`, `src/ui/Input.h:123`).

### 4.6 Persistence

| What | Where | Life |
|---|---|---|
| HEAD, MARK, HOUR, DAYS, RCNT, the TRKS delta | `/.player/stats.bin`, and RAM | Saved at a boot with new plays, before a rotation over plays not yet saved (in the end's flush too, with 1 s of its budget left), and by `Hs`; RAM in between |
| TRKS rows as of the last compaction | `/.player/stats-<gen>.bin` | Until the next compaction |
| The lists, the ids, the album bits | RAM | Remade after each boot and each update's Live |
| A shelf's window | RAM, a shelf at a time | The session. Most played starts on All time |
| NVS | Nothing (S6 adds `clock/tz`) | |

## 5. The data

### 5.1 The pipeline, and where the stats join it

As built (READ):
1. PlaybackController's word goes to the Tracker, a ring of 32 plays in PSRAM (`lib/core/PlayLog.h:362-427`). `tick()` counts playedMs and the pauses (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:593-618`). Plays under 1 s are dropped; a 33rd waiting play is lost (`:567-591`).
2. `stepPlays` (`src/main.cpp:3499-3518`) calls `PlayHistory::loop` every pass. It waits for the WriteGate (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:767-791`) and asks nothing while the card is held or away (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:181-192`).
3. Before Ready: a piece of the boot's scan, 128 slots (`src/app/PlayHistory.h:91`). After: one `Outbox::writeOne` a pass (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:214-217`): the f_stat, the slot, `Journal::append` (seq to NVS first, encode, write, f_sync: `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:522-544`).
4. Written: `done()` counts it, calls `env_.tried`, then `pop()` (`:633-647`).

The stats add, all on the loop:
- **The live fold**, at `Outbox::done()` for a Written play only (5.6).
- **The boot's fold**, inside `Journal::scan()` for each valid slot (5.6).
- **The save**, inside `finishScan()` before the trim, and in `rotate()` before `plays.1.jnl` leaves the log's names (5.5).
- **The ranking and the per-track read**, on the card worker, after Ready (6.8).
- **The walk** that names the hashes, on the loop in slices (6.2).

### 5.2 `/.player/stats.bin`

A card container (`docs/METADATA.md` 2.4; `lib/core/CardContainer.h`):
- magic `MPST`, unused by any other file (READ: no other fourcc in `lib/core` or `src` spells it);
- major 1, minor 0;
- headerBytes 40, no type header;
- generation: the save count, from 1;
- cardId 0. The stats are never keyed to MSMF's cardId: a new set-up of `/.mstream` changes it while `/.player` stays (2.11's rows, `docs/METADATA.md:1789-1790`). The file is the card's because it is on the card.

Its sections, in this order. Every one is REQUIRED (a reader that doesn't know one leaves the file alone: 5.4).

| Type | Kind | Stride | Holds |
|---|---|---|---|
| `HEAD` | blob, 128 B | — | The totals and the file's state |
| `MARK` | array | 24 | The highest seq folded, per (deviceId, epoch) |
| `HOUR` | array | 8 | Each UTC hour with a slot that counts, the newest 840 |
| `DAYS` | array | 16 | Each UTC day with one, the newest 400 |
| `RCNT` | array | 12 | Plays per (UTC day, track), the last 32 days, at most 4,096 rows |
| `TRKD` | array | 24 | The TRKS delta: rows changed since the base |

All little-endian.

**Retention**, at each save:
- HOUR and DAYS are capped by rows, the oldest going first: HOUR at most 840 rows (35 days of every hour), DAYS at most 400. Neither is ever pruned by date, so no clock (unset, wrong or foreign) can prune a row of them. DAYS is primary data the log can't rebuild.
- RCNT keeps 32 days, at most 4,096 rows. Its days count back from the earlier of today and the newest dated day folded. With the clock unset or not believed (5.9), nothing is pruned by date at that save. A row newer than the reference is kept.
- So one foreign slot dated 2099 (believable: 4.1) moves nothing: sinceDay is never after today (4.1). A clock set a year ahead for one save, then corrected, costs RCNT's windows up to a month, never a lifetime figure, an hour or a day.
- Two cases still set a future sinceDay, for good: a board clock already fast at the first save, and, with the clock unset at that save, a foreign slot dated ahead (nothing then to cap it by). Then the first month's states last until the clock catches up.

**HEAD** (128 B):

| Off | Size | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | plays |
| 8 | 8 | skips |
| 16 | 8 | listenedMs |
| 24 | 8 | slots: slots that count, folded |
| 32 | 48 | per source, "you", shuffle, Auto DJ: plays u32, skips u32, listenedMs u64 (16 B each) |
| 80 | 4 | undated: plays that weren't dated |
| 84 | 2 | sinceDay (0xFFFF: unset) |
| 86 | 2 | flags: bit 0 sinceDay came from the sync's commitTime; bit 1 a base was lost (5.4); the rest 0 |
| 88 | 4 | round: the last fold round given out (5.6) |
| 92 | 4 | baseGen: the base file's number (0: none) |
| 96 | 4 | baseRows |
| 100 | 4 | baseCrc: the base file's header CRC |
| 104 | 4 | twice: slots the boot's folds met a second time (a diagnostic) |
| 108 | 4 | gap: seqs ACKS covers above MARK that the log no longer holds, added up at each boot that finds some (plays a firmware without stats trimmed; an estimate). That boot's MARK then covers ACKS's seq, so the next boot doesn't count them again (5.6) |
| 112 | 16 | reserved, 0 |

**MARK** row: deviceId 6 B at 0; 2 B reserved; epoch u64 at 8; seq u32 at 16 (the highest folded, or counted in HEAD's gap); 4 B reserved. Strictly increasing by (deviceId's bytes, epoch). MARK rows are never dropped: one row (24 B) for each (deviceId, epoch) ever folded on this card. ACKS's leave-out rule (`docs/METADATA.md:1071-1073`) does not apply to MARK: the scan can miss slots that are on the card (an open the card fails reads as a missing file, a failed read ends a file: `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:256-261, 302-306`), and a row dropped then would let the next boot fold those slots again. Never capped at ACKS's 16 rows.

**HOUR** row: hour u24 at 0 (UTC hours since 2026-01-01; 16.7 M hours); plays u8 at 3 (saturating); listenedMs u32 at 4 (a slot's whole time goes to the hour it started in). Strictly increasing by hour.

**DAYS** row: day u16 at 0; plays u16 at 2; skips u16 at 4; Auto DJ's plays u8 at 6 and skips u8 at 7; listenedMs u32 at 8; shuffle's plays u8 at 12 and skips u8 at 13; 2 B reserved. "You" is the rest. u8 counts saturate. Strictly increasing by day. This is the spec's DSRC with the day's totals added.

**RCNT** row: day u16 at 0; plays u16 at 2; path hash u64 at 4. Only plays. Strictly increasing by (day, hash). Past 4,096 rows whole days go, oldest first, until 4,096 or fewer rows are left (a day of more than 4,096 tracks goes whole too), and the 30-day window then holds fewer days (its tray cell says how many: 7.6).

**TRKS row** (TRKD's, and the base's TRKS):

| Off | Size | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 8 | pathHash (`docs/METADATA.md` 2.3.3; the slot's) |
| 8 | 2 | plays, saturating |
| 10 | 2 | skips, saturating |
| 12 | 4 | firstAt: the earliest dated counted play's time (4.1); 0 counted plays, none dated; 0xFFFFFFFF none (skips only) |
| 16 | 4 | lastAt: the latest dated counted play's time; 0 none |
| 20 | 3 | lastOrd: the fold round of the latest counted play |
| 23 | 1 | flags: bits 0-1 the source of the play firstAt keeps (0 you, 1 shuffle, 2 Auto DJ); bits 2-7 0 |

Strictly increasing by hash. A row exists for a track with a play or a skip.

**Merging two rows of one hash**, `a ⊕ b` with `a` the older (the base before the delta; a row before a slot). Every rule is order-free, so a row saved from live folds (a rotation's save, `Hs`) and the same row folded at a boot (newest file first) are the same but for lastOrd, a round (5.6: a live fold's is HEAD.round + 2, a boot's + 1), which the proof compares by order:
- plays, skips: sums, saturating;
- firstAt: the earlier dated time. 0 (undated) only when neither side has a dated one. 0xFFFFFFFF (none) loses to both. The source flag goes with the time kept; on equal times, two undated included, the smaller source code wins;
- lastAt, lastOrd: the larger.

A slot is folded into its row as the row of that one slot:
- a play: plays 1, firstAt and lastAt its time (firstAt 0 when undated, lastAt 0), lastOrd its round, its source;
- a skip: skips 1, firstAt 0xFFFFFFFF, lastAt 0, lastOrd 0.

### 5.3 The base file, `/.player/stats-<gen>.bin`

- `<gen>` is HEAD's baseGen in 8 lowercase hex digits.
- The same frame (magic `MPST`, major 1), generation baseGen, one REQUIRED section `TRKS` (array, stride 24), rows strictly increasing by hash.
- It is written under a name `stats.bin` doesn't name; a stale file there (left by a `stats.bin` deleted or corrupt, or by a base lost: 5.4, 5.7, which start baseGen again at 0) is replaced, as the reference does (created with `FA_CREATE_ALWAYS`). `stats.bin` names it, so the rename of `stats.bin` is the one commit point.
- So a compaction never writes the name the card's `stats.bin` names. After a base lost, baseGen starts again at 0, and its next, 1, may be the lost one's name: a cut after the create would leave a base shorter than its header under the name `stats.bin` names, Unread at every boot (5.4), the stats off for good. After a held tmp that compacted is promoted (5.5), the name is the held base's, and a failed save would lose it. That compaction waits for the next boot's save, whose `stats.bin` names another base or none. (As built, S1 C7: `tools/statsref.py` first compacted over a lost base 1's name; it now skips as the firmware does, and the `base-other` fixture is two boots, the second compacting over that file.)
- **Compaction** writes `base ⊕ delta`, a merge of two sorted streams, to `stats-<gen+1>.bin`. Then `stats.bin` names it with an empty TRKD. Then the old base is removed.
- When: at a boot's save, while nothing plays, when the delta is over 32 KB or over a quarter of the base (with no base, any delta: the first boot's save writes one). Forced past 96 KB, but only at a safe point: nothing playing, or the heard track with 20 s or more left and no seek in the last 2 s (the update step's own safe point, `src/main.cpp:3559-3563`). Otherwise at the next boot's save. A play waiting for the headphones, or a restart asked, counts as playing here, as the safe point counts it (`LibraryUpdate::safePoint()`: its start, or the restart, mustn't wait behind a compaction; as built, the S1 review, where the glue had passed PlayState::Playing alone). The boot's save runs inside `finishScan()`, one synchronous call after a WriteGate checked once at the pass's start (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:767-791`: TrackEnd only under 5 s left), so the gate alone doesn't keep a compaction of 0.8-1.6 s out of the gapless window.
- A cut anywhere leaves `stats.bin` naming a whole base. A file it doesn't name is `stats-<gen−1>` or `stats-<gen+1>`, and goes at the next boot after a Loaded read (5.4). Nothing lists the folder.

### 5.4 Reading at the boot

In `PlayHistory::begin()`, before `journal_->begin()` (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:119-120`):

1. `tagstore::settle(fs, kStatsNames, &check)`, names `{"/.player/stats.bin", "/.player/stats.tmp", "/.player/stats"}` (twins `stats.xl1`-`.xl9`). `check` is `StatsCheck`, a real TmpCheck (`lib/core/TagStore.h:314-323`): the frame and every section's CRC. A null check takes a lone tmp for torn and removes it (`lib/core/TagStore.cpp:454, 463`): after a cut between the remove of `stats.bin` and the rename, that tmp is the only copy. `StatsCheck` answers:
   - **Whole** for a frame whose checks hold, and also for one whose open says Major or UnknownRequired: that tmp is promoted, then read below as Newer and left alone (5.12). `IdxCheck` counts an Outdated `library.idx` as whole the same way (`lib/core/LibraryUpdate.cpp:39-61`). Answered Torn, a lone tmp of a newer stats firmware (a downgrade, or an older second player: O2) would be its whole history deleted.
   - **Unknown** for a read the card fails, records that are damaged (`File::damaged()`: never readable, but the tmp may be the only copy), or no memory for the check: the tmp is kept.
   - **Torn** only for a frame that fails its own checks.

   Settle `Unread` or `Kept`: the verdict is Unread.
2. Open `stats.bin` with `openRead(path, &cardFailed)` (`lib/core/TagStore.h:206-215`). A null with the card failed: Unread. A null otherwise is Absent only when `look()` says Gone, the card read and no `stats.bin` there (`:233-237`). Unknown (the file system's records of the way to it damaged) is Unread.
3. `Container::open`, each section's CRC checked as it is loaded (`Stream::crc()`, `lib/core/CardContainer.h:237`: one pass, not `checkCrc` and then a load). As built (the rework, 5.14's R4): the one pass loads HEAD and MARK into the fold and only checks HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD as they stream (nothing else of the file is kept in RAM), and every section's CRC is judged before any order (SectionCrc, then Shape for a HEAD under 128 B, then the rows' order), as `tools/statsref.py` reads. MARK's first 16 rows are in the glue's object; for a card whose MARK has more than 8 rows the boot's read carves a block of its rows and 8 more (the store's hook: PSRAM), kept for the session, and a carve that fails is a read with no memory (stats Off). Its `Why` (`lib/core/CardContainer.h:91-136`) decides. `open()` returns Short (`lib/core/CardContainer.cpp:89, 105`), Io, Magic, Major, HeaderBytes (`:102`), SectionCount (`:103`), FileBytes, HeaderCrc, Layout, UnknownRequired, Shape and Missing, a REQUIRED section that isn't there (`:165`). The load adds SectionCrc, Counts and the rows' order. Why's Missing is not the verdict Absent below:

| Why | Verdict | What follows |
|---|---|---|
| Ok, and the rows in order | Loaded | The fold goes on above its MARK |
| Io; FatRecords (`File::damaged()`, `lib/core/TagStore.h:143`) | Unread | At the boot, Io is read again after 100, 300 and 1,000 ms (`tagstore::kBootWaitsMs`, `lib/core/TagStore.h:349`); FatRecords once, as cardroot's rule has it: no read again changes it (`lib/core/CardRoot.cpp:31-36`). Still unread: stats Off this session; no fold, no save, **no trim** |
| Major, UnknownRequired | Newer | Stats Off this session; the file left as it is; no trim |
| Magic, Short, HeaderBytes, SectionCount, FileBytes, HeaderCrc, Layout, Missing (a required section absent), Shape, SectionCrc, Counts, any other Why, or a row out of order | Corrupt | Read once more after 100 ms. Still corrupt: renamed `stats.bad` (an older one removed first), then Absent. `stats.bad` never shares a chain (only the twins are names 2.12.6's rule knows, and it would one day free one that `stats.bad` holds): with another entry on `stats.bin`'s chain, `stats.bin` goes to a twin instead, and an older `stats.bad` on it too. A rename or remove the card fails: Unread |
| — (step 2: the card says there is no `stats.bin`) | Absent | A fresh start: MARK empty, so every slot on the card is folded |

4. Loaded: the base it names is opened with `openRead(path, &cardFailed)` and read whole, as the reference's `load()` does: its header, then its rows' CRC, order and count against baseRows, in one pass, no row kept (24 B a played track: 53 KB at 2,214, 467 KB at 19,478; as built, S1 C7, where this step had its header alone read). A card failure (cardFailed, Io), or rows whose CRC fails, is read again at `kBootWaitsMs`; damaged records once. If one persists, the verdict is Unread: stats Off this session, with no fold, no save and no trim. A base is lost (flags bit 1, baseGen 0) only when the card answers that it isn't there (`look()` Gone), or when its header reads whole with a CRC other than baseCrc. A base shorter than its header doesn't read whole: Unread, as one of any other length that fails its frame. Then the rows before it are lost and the totals stay, and on that boot no `stats-<n>.bin` is removed. A base whose header is the one named but whose rows fail their CRC or their order is a card fault too: Unread, never a lost base. `stats-<gen±1>.bin` are removed only after the named base checked whole.
5. The root's commitTime is kept for sinceDay (`lib/core/CardRoot.cpp:111-114` gains it in `cardroot::Root`).

A `stats.bin` (or a base) the card failed to read is never an absent one. Saving a log-only aggregate over it would erase the history the trims already removed (the queue's precedent: `src/app/QueueStore.cpp:181-186`). With MARK unknown, nothing is safe to trim.

**At a remount** (`remounted()`, 5.7): one read, no waits. The boot's waits are for the boot, where "nothing else is held up" (`lib/core/TagStore.h:345-349`); a remount runs on the loop (`src/main.cpp:3242`), perhaps minutes into the first track. The remount drops the folds at once (the records and any overflow); the read itself (its work memory, `stats.bin` and its base read whole) runs at the scan's first piece (`Glue::reserve()`), in a pass the write gate lets through, where Auto DJ's join isn't Working and no library update is asked, not in the card guard's step, which runs every pass whatever the join or the gate (`src/main.cpp:4043`; as built, the S1 review). It carves nothing to keep: a MARK with more rows than the object and the boot's block hold (another player wrote the card while it was out) is a read with no memory, the stats Off for the session (no fold, no save, no trim), never a MARK cut short, which the next save would write without those players' rows (5.14). Unread there: no fold, no save and no trim, and the read is tried again at the next remount. A read that leaves the stats off (Unread, Newer, no memory), here or at the boot, holds nothing but the object (and MARK's block, if the boot carved one). A `stats.bin` that reads corrupt there is not renamed on one read: Unread, left for the next boot's two. While the orderly end flushes (a remount inside `PlayHistory::flush()`, `src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:288-290`, within its 3 s, `src/app/PlayHistory.h:101`), or when the flush starts with a remount's read not made yet: no read at all; the folds are dropped and no save follows.

**No read after a library update** (the rework, 5.14): nothing of the stats is given back during one, so there is nothing to read again. (S1 as first built gave its resident blocks back during each update and read `stats.bin` again after it, a read the card failed made again the boot's waits apart: 6.7's history.)

### 5.5 Writing: the save, and its order against the trim

**When.**
- At the boot's scan's end, in `finishScan()` (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:318-347`): after the boot rules and before the trim loop (`:332`). Also on the path where `storeBoots` fails (`:323-326`): the fold is whole there, and a save is still right. No slot exists yet (append is Off before Ready, `:523`), so nothing can change under the save. As built (the rework, 5.14): when the fold took more than 96 records (the overflow) and this save doesn't land (a step the card failed, no work memory, the card away), it is tried again at later passes the write gate lets through, `tagstore::kBootWaitsMs` (100, 300 and 1,000 ms) apart, four tries in all, as the boot's (a compaction allowed), the overflow held meanwhile; a slot written meanwhile goes into its last chunk's room, and a rotation's save in that window merges the overflow too. After the fourth try, a refusal, or the orderly end's flush, the session is behind (below) and the chunks go.
- In `rotate()` (`:411-463`), after `unread()` (`:423-433`) and before the `shared()` question (`:434`), so it covers the twin rename (`:439-441`) as well as the delete (`:437`). It saves when `unread()`'s pass over `plays.1.jnl` (`:349-375`) met a play slot the card's MARK doesn't cover, or, in a session whose scan was whole, couldn't read the file whole. The rotation goes on whatever the save does: plays are never held back for the stats.
  - With the boot's save done, a rotation saves nothing: the file it deletes is the one that save covered, and `plays.jnl`, holding this session's plays, is renamed, not deleted (`:437, 451`). The rotation's save runs at a second rotation in one session (6,553 more plays), or after a boot whose save failed or was skipped.
  - In the orderly end's flush too: `Outbox::flush()` can rotate (`writeOne()` at `:750`, `append()` at `:701`, `place()` at `:516`, `rotate()` at `:505`), and a power-off or restart always follows the flush (`src/main.cpp:278-281`). There the rotation's save runs when at least 1 s of `kFlushBudgetMs` (`src/app/PlayHistory.h:101`) is left (a rotation's save never compacts). Otherwise the plays in the deleted file are lost to the stats.
- `Hs`, the console's (the rotation's path), at a pass the write gate lets through once no play waits for its slot that the card may take: those are written first, one a pass, each folded live, so its save holds every play the gate held back (`statsglue::turnOf()`; S1's device round 1, run H: at the pass the gate opened, Hs had saved "nothing new" with 4 plays waiting for that gate). A play whose try the card failed, its 5 s not over, isn't waited for.
- **The records' save** (the rework, 5.14: `Kind::Records`), due once the records number 64 (`statsfold::kSaveAt`), whether the boot's fold or a live slot brought them there, at the first pass the play log's write gate lets through while it is due, before that pass's slot (`statsglue::turnOf()`: a save due, then a slot, then `Hs`), so it holds exactly the records taken by then. It waits while the card is away and while a library update's fence is up, until the records reach 96 (`statsfold::kRecords`), when it runs whatever the fence (the Tracker's backlog and the plays that end during the fence's 13-30 s can reach it). It is refused while the orderly end flushes (its 3 s are the plays'; at most 95 records then wait for the next boot's fold). One that doesn't land is due again only once the records pass the count it was tried at (`recordsTried_`); any save that lands or is held resets that. Never a compaction.
- **Not at a library update** (the rework): nothing of the stats is given back, so nothing is saved first. (S1 as first built saved first at its give-back, at the update's memory check or its fence, waiting for the write gate and for the plays queued for their slots: 6.7's history.)

It is skipped, returning false:
- the boot's save, while the orderly end flushes (an "ending" flag set as `flush()` starts, before its remount at `src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:288-290`, where 5.4's no-read holds, and so before `outbox_->flush()` at `:307`; as built, S1 C9, where this said "before `:307`" alone): the 3 s budget is the plays' (`src/app/PlayHistory.h:101`), and `Outbox::flush` can reach `finishScan()` (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:743`). The ending flag holds back the boot's save and, since the rework, the records' (a rotation's has its 1 s rule); skipping them loses nothing (the next boot folds again);
- when the card is away, or another card is in the slot (`cardAway()`, `sectordisk::guard().foreign`): one card's stats never go onto another;
- when stats are Off, or the fold ran out of memory;
- every save of a session whose scan was partial (5.6);
- for the rest of a session that is **behind** its log (`Refused::Behind`, the rework: RAM lacks slots the log holds above the card's MARK, so a later save would raise MARK past them, unfolded): the 97th live record with the records full after a save that didn't land or was refused; a boot overflow whose save didn't land in its four tries, or was refused, or met the orderly end; a partial scan's overflow, or a fold out of memory's past 96 records (dropped at the fold's end); a held tmp lost before its promotion, or one no longer ours; a section of `stats.bin` damaged under the session (its CRC failing under the header RAM stands on: `Saved::Damaged`, `H` naming it). The next boot folds what the log still holds; a rotation meanwhile deletes those in `plays.1.jnl`, its own save refused by this rule, so they are lost (below). (S1 as first built also refused every save while its blocks were given back to a library update, and was behind after a give-back that left RAM short: 6.7's history.)
- when the card's `stats.bin` is no longer the copy RAM stands on. The glue keeps what it read at the boot (or a remount's read) or last wrote: no `stats.bin` (Absent, nothing saved yet), or its generation and header CRC. In step 1, after `settle()`, it reads `stats.bin`'s header and compares: a file where none was expected, none where one was, or another header is a change. A change refuses this save and every later one this session, `[listen]` says why, and the next boot reads the card's copy. The card guard's "same card" is its size and two sectors' CRCs (`src/storage/SectorDisk.h:22-35`; `src/app/PlayHistory.h:84-88`): a card that went into another player and came back passes it. Without this check this RAM would be saved over that player's newer `stats.bin` (its plays, and the base it compacted to, lost: the next boot would find its base gone and drop every per-track row), or over a `/.player` deleted on a PC.

With nothing new since the card's copy, it returns true and writes nothing.

**The steps.** The queue's commit (`src/app/QueueStore.cpp:473-500`), not `LibraryUpdate::save`'s (`lib/core/LibraryUpdate.cpp:400-401`), which removes the tmp after a failed `replace()`, possibly the only copy (`replace()` may already have removed the file: `lib/core/TagStore.cpp:493-495`).
1. A tmp held by an earlier save (step 5) is promoted first with `replace()`, as `QueueStore::settleTemp` does (`src/app/QueueStore.cpp:456-467`; `replaceFile` at `:465`): false, the tmp still held. As built (the S1 review), in a call of its own, `Store::promote()`, that the glue makes before it carves the save's work memory: the promotion's frames (`replace()`, a twin's rename) are then never under the save's (the loop's stack, 11). Only while its header is still the one written: another's (a player that had the card meanwhile) is left to `settle()`. The file then written takes the promoted one's generation + 1 (a held tmp promoted is a save that landed), and a remount's read promotes it first too (its `settle()` would drop it while `stats.bin` is there). Then `settle()` with the real check, the header check above, then `prepareTmp()`. A question the card fails, or a `settle()` that fails (a remove, or a rename to a twin): false, nothing done.
2. Compaction, when due (5.3): the new base written, closed, its size checked. A failure leaves the base as it was, and the save goes on. As built (the rework, 5.14's R4): due is decided on the merged TRKD's count, step 3's; the compaction runs between the counting and the writing pass, merging three sorted streams (the base, the card's TRKD and the records, the older side first on equal hashes) into `stats-<gen+1>.bin`; a read the card fails in it, or rows failing their CRC or order, is a compaction failed (the new base removed), and the writing pass reads TRKD again.
3. `stats.tmp` created and written through a `ContainerWriter` in 4 KB aligned pieces (`lib/core/TagStore.h:184-195`), then closed. As built (the rework): nothing of the card's rows is kept in RAM, so the file is a streaming merge of the card's `stats.bin` with the records, sorted in place for each section's key (a heap sort). A counting pass reads HEAD to TRKD, each section's CRC taken as it streams, merging HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD with the records by ⊕ (5.2) and counting the rows each keeps after its cut (HOUR's newest 840, DAYS' newest 400, RCNT's 32 days and then 4,096 rows by whole days, merged a second time when its count passes 4,096, to find the first day kept). The writing pass writes HEAD and MARK from RAM and the four sections merged again, each section's size known from the first pass; one whose merge gives another count is a save failed (step "rows"), the tmp removed. A read the card fails in either pass is a save failed, tried again; a section whose CRC fails under the header RAM stands on is `Saved::Damaged` (behind, above).
4. The tmp's size on the card must equal what was written (`QueueStore::tmpWhole`, `src/app/QueueStore.cpp:372-392`). Not: the tmp removed, false.
5. `replace()`. False: the tmp is kept, whole, and held; the next save promotes it first (step 1). A boot's `settle()` takes a whole tmp when `stats.bin` is gone, and drops one while `stats.bin` is there (`lib/core/TagStore.cpp:454, 463`): a held tmp not promoted before a restart is lost, and with it the plays of a `plays.1.jnl` a rotation deleted after that save (13). As built (the rework, 5.14): a held tmp holds every record, so the records are emptied and any overflow given back, and RAM's HEAD takes the plan's (its baseGen, baseRows, baseCrc and round), but the card's MARK in RAM (what `covered()` answers by) stays the one before until the promotion; the held tmp counts as something new, and the next save of any kind promotes it (`Store::promote()`: Promoted, the card's MARK then the held copy's; Behind, the tmp lost or no longer ours; Changed; Fault) and merges only the records taken since onto it.
