6. True: the card's MARK is now the one written, and an old base is removed. As built (the rework): the records are emptied and any overflow given back.

The `ContainerWriter` is about 2.1 KB (`lib/core/CardContainer.h:281-313`: 64 section entries, two u32[64], a 256 B header). The loop's stack is 8 KB, with 2.5-3.8 KB left at its lowest (`docs/METADATA.md:6322`; `docs/AUTODJ.md:1802-1803`). So the writer and the save's buffers are in PSRAM, never on the loop's stack. As built (the rework, 5.14's R4): the work memory (`statsfile::kWorkBytes`: the writer, its plan, two containers, two row readers, the records' cursor and the merge's rows, then a 4 KB buffer and two of 2 KB) is 11,456 B on the device, carved for each read and save and given back in the same call, beside a 4,148 B `FatFile` for each file open (`FatFs::open()`'s `psramNew<FatFile>`): 19,752 B in three blocks for a save, 23,900 B in four for a compaction.

**The trim.**
- A log file goes when today's rule holds (every play acknowledged, at least one, a play last, no twin on its chain: `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:292-297, 310-311, 332-344`) **and** every play slot in it is at or under the card's MARK for its (deviceId, epoch).
- After a save that succeeded, that MARK covers everything the scan read.
- After one that failed, or none (a partial scan, a changed `stats.bin`), it is the MARK read at the boot. So a full card still frees the files earlier boots folded.
- With stats on but no MARK known (Unread, Newer), nothing is trimmed. The log stays bounded: the rotation holds it to two files of 6,553 slots (`lib/core/PlayLog.h:61-64`).
- A fold that ran out of memory can't be saved; the trim then follows the MARK read at the boot, as after a failed save.
- With no stats (a `Folds` of null), the trim is today's.
- No ACKS, no trim, as today.

**Why not the worker.**
- No window: `finishScan()` is one synchronous call (1.1).
- The worker is busy with the post-sync rebuild just then (`docs/METADATA.md:1066-1068`).
- Its 7.5 KB stack is internal RAM, made for each burst of work (`src/app/CardWorker.h:38-61`).
- Folds while it wrote would need snapshots.

**Why not pieces across passes:** a state between the scan and Ready drops plays (1.1).

**What a failed save costs.** Nothing is lost: the card's MARK stays, and the next boot folds the same slots again. The exceptions have one shape: a rotation deletes (or moves to a twin) a `plays.1.jnl` holding plays the card's MARK doesn't cover, and no save lands before the restart. Then those plays are in no file the scan reads. It happens when:
- the rotation's save failed (a full or failing card), or its tmp was held and not promoted before the restart (step 5);
- the stats are Off this session (Unread, Newer, no memory for the fold, the folds dropped while ending);
- this session's saves are refused (a partial scan, a changed `stats.bin`, behind: above);
- the rotation came in the end's flush with under 1 s of its budget left;
- (the rework, 5.14) a full log whose boot save fails all four of its tries: a full log's `plays.jnl` is at its cap, so the session's first play rotates, its save refused (behind), and `plays.1.jnl`'s uncovered plays are lost, up to 6,553 (statsref's `boot-overflow-full-rotation`: 128 at the fixtures' cap of 128; 0 when the second try lands). It needs a fault that lasts past the boot's waits (about 1.4 s) and clears before that play's end; one that lasts through the rotation loses them as S1 as first built did too. The owner's default: documented, not closed;
- (the rework) a session behind its log otherwise (above: the records full after a save that didn't land, a boot overflow not saved in a log that isn't full, a held tmp lost, a section damaged): a rotation then needs `plays.jnl`'s room in plays (statsref's `records-behind-rotation`: 64 at the cap of 64).

S1 as first built had two more, both its give-back's: a rotation while the blocks were given back, its own save unlanded (`update-rotation-unsaved`), and one after a take-back that left RAM behind (`update-behind-rotation`); both closed with the give-back (6.7's history).

13 lists it.

**What not keeping the data costs** (the rework, 5.14; 13). A section of `stats.bin` damaged after the boot's read can't be healed: every save reads it, so the session's saves stop at its first failure (behind), and the next boot reads the file Corrupt (5.4), renames it `stats.bad` and starts again from what the log still holds (HEAD's totals, HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD lost); S1 as first built wrote RAM's copy over it at the next save. Behind stops the live records and the saves for the session: a card that fills or fails mid-session shows fewer plays until the next boot. Each save reads `stats.bin` twice, and the records' save is a loop pause once each 64 plays. (S1 as first built's "What a give-back costs the screen" went with the give-back: 6.7's history.)

### 5.6 The fold

**The live fold.**
- At `Outbox::done()`, only when `t.did == Did::Written`: after `append()` or `again()` said Ok, so synced (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:711-714`). It runs before `tracker_.pop()` (`:641-642`), so `p` is still the ring's oldest.
- Through a new `Outbox::Env::written(const Slot&, const Played&)`, beside `tried()` (`lib/core/PlayLog.h:486`). It has an empty default body, so test Envs compile unchanged.
- `done()` also runs for Dropped plays: Gone, Off, Nvs, Blocked, and given up after 3 tries (`:649-658, 676-679, 718-729`). Those are never folded.
- The fold takes `t.slot` exactly as encoded (`:702, 710`): `append()` fills its seq, epoch, deviceId and bootSeq (`:536-541`), and on a retry it is still the first try's slot. It is never dated again from `boardclock::now()` or `p.startMono`, so the next boot's fold of the same bytes gives the same result.
- From S2 (the lists and the map use ids; S1's fold is hash-keyed and asks for none): `p.file.path` names the file, and `LibraryIndex::findTrack()` gives its id (`lib/core/LibraryIndex.h:395-397`) when the index is readable; behind the fence it is not asked.
- RAM only: no card write and no NVS write for a play.
- As built (the rework, 5.14): the play is a record (20 B: its path hash, its time in seconds from 2026, 0 undated, its playedMs, a play, a skip or neither, its source, its round) among the glue's object's 96; while a boot's overflow waits for its save, in its last chunk's room. A live slot never carves: with the records full (a save that didn't land or was refused) the 97th isn't folded, and the session is behind (5.5). (S1 as first built folded each play into RCNT's and TRKD's rows in RAM, and kept a ring of 32 slots for the plays written while its blocks were given back to a library update: 6.7's history.)

**The boot's fold.**
- `Journal::Config` gains a `Folds*` (null: no stats, today's behaviour; 9.5's C4 lists its calls).
- `startScan()` (`:153-165`) calls `scanBegan(again)`. A scan begun again after a remount (`:167-174`) has the glue read `stats.bin` again, dropping the folds made before. Without that, every slot read before the remount would count twice.
- Each slot `decode` says Ok goes to `slot(s, file)`, after the damaged-slot skip (`:285`). Drop notes (kind 2) are skipped by the stats: their playedMs is a dropped count and their seq 0 (`lib/core/PlayLog.h:82`; `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:22-28`).
- A slot that counts is folded when its seq is above the MARK read at the boot for its (deviceId, epoch), from every deviceId (O2).
- **A partial scan.** Today the scan takes an open the card failed for a missing file (`fs_.open()` null, the next name: `:256-261`) and ends a file at a failed read (`:270-275, 302-306`). Raising MARK past slots it never read would lose them for good (once ACKS covers them, the trim deletes the file). So with folds the scan opens each log file with `openRead(name, &cardFailed)`; an open the card failed, or a read that failed, calls `Folds::partial()`. So does an open that finds no file with the card not at fault when `look()` then says Unknown, not Gone: FatFs's FR_INT_ERR, the file system's records on the way to the file damaged, where the file may well be there and a later disk check makes it readable (as built, the S1 review: one `f_stat` for each log name the open found nothing at, with folds only; 5.4 step 2 takes the same answer for `stats.bin` as Unread). A `/.player` whose directory chain is broken then keeps the saves off until a disk check. After a partial scan no save runs this session: the boot's, a rotation's and `Hs` all return false. The trim follows the MARK read at the boot, and keeps the file not read whole (as today's scan keeps a file it couldn't judge); the next boot folds again. A partial scan counts no gap (below): the slots it didn't read may hold those seqs. The same holds within a session: live folds raise MARK in RAM above an unread tail, and that MARK is never written.
- **Each slot once.** In one file, one device and epoch's seqs only rise, so a slot at or under the seq last folded for its row from that file is skipped. Across files, each row keeps the seq range each file read so far gave it, at most 10. A slot inside another file's range is skipped and counted in `twice`. Two files on one chain (a rename cut that `settle()` couldn't part: `:227-243`) give the same slots twice, and this keeps one. A conflict (one key, different bytes) keeps the first read; mStream's reader drops both (2.20.6 item 3), which a log with this seq rule can't produce. The reference (`tools/statsref.py`) keys each slot by (deviceId, epoch, seq): the first read kept, one met again from another file counted in `twice`. The ranges give the same on any log a device writes, and on every fixture: each fixture's log files keep one device and epoch's seqs in order, and `tools/test_statsref.py` checks that, and that the ranges choose the reference's slots, on every boot.
- **The gap.** For each ACKS row above the MARK read at the boot, the seqs it covers that no valid play slot of the log holds (a firmware without stats trimmed them) are added to HEAD's gap, and MARK rises to ACKS's seq: those seqs are gone for good (ACKS covers only seqs the software read from this card, and seqs only rise), and a MARK left under them would count them again at every boot. The rows are the ones the root's read keeps (`cardroot::Root::acks`: at most `msmf::Acks::kMax`, 16, the device's own first), as the journal's trim takes them; a row past them is counted at a boot that keeps it (this player's later, or another player's). `tools/statsref.py` takes every row it is given, and its fixtures never give more than 16 (the S1 review).
- **Order** comes from the slot, never from the scan: firstAt and lastAt are times; a play's round is the boot's round (below).
- **The cost** is CPU and RAM only: no read beyond the scan's own. As built (the rework, 5.14): a slot is appended as a record, nothing is looked up or merged while folding, so no index and no sorted insert. Each save sorts the records in place for each section's key (a heap sort: `std::sort`'s introsort recursed up to about 2 KB on the loop's stack under the save) and merges them, up to 17 sorted runs at a boot (the object's and 16 chunks'), with the card's rows as it streams (5.5). The rank (S2) reads `stats.bin` and takes a copy of the records (5.14, "The screens"). (S1 as first built kept TRKD's and RCNT's rows in RAM, in chunks of 16,392 B, with a transient open-addressing index during the boot's fold, binary searches and a sorted tail for live folds, and HOUR and DAYS carved at their caps: 6.7's history.)
- **Memory** (the rework, 5.14). The glue's object, carved once in setup and never freed, holds HEAD, MARK's first 16 rows (28 B each: RAM's seq, the card's, and the one a held tmp holds) and the 96 records: 3,200 B on the device, under a 3,584 B `static_assert`. A card with more than 8 MARK rows has a block of its rows and 8 more from the boot's read (5.4). The boot's fold carves, at the scan's first piece, its ranges' block (the per-file seq ranges, 80 B a MARK row, at most 10 files; the ACKS rows above MARK, 112 B each, at most 16, for the gap): 1,284 B for 16 MARK rows and no ACKS row above them, 3,076 B at most (4 B over and aligned to 8: the ACKS rows hold a u64, and PSRAM's heap gives 4-byte-aligned blocks); and, past 96 records, overflow chunks of 16,384 B (819 records, carving 16,900 B), at most 16 (a full never-synced log, 13,106 slots: 262,144 B, the heap's peak with the boot save's work memory 273,632 B on the host, MEASURED-HOST, where S1 as first built's fold peaked at 396,744 B). Both are carved at the top of PSRAM's largest free block through Auto DJ's spacers keeping 64 KB free (`kTopMarginBytes` in app/ListeningStats, Auto DJ's `kJoinMarginBytes` figure), only in a pass where Auto DJ's join isn't Working and, before the scan's first piece, no library update is asked (otherwise the scan waits that pass). The fold carves a chunk itself at the record past the 96, in the same piece of a pass that may carve (the scan reserves no look-ahead, `statsglue::kReserveAhead`: R6 had found the first piece's reserve of 128 slots carving one at every boot, which the rework review took out), so only a fold past the records holds chunks, a full log's, once a card. A card that goes away mid-scan ends the fold as a partial scan's, its blocks given back (`Glue::scanAway()`). The ranges' block goes at the fold's end (before the boot's save carves its work memory; `ready()` after a partial scan; a remount's `scanBegan(true)`), with the chunks no record needs; the rest when the boot's save lands or is held, or after its fourth try. A partial scan's overflow, or a fold out of memory's past 96 records, is dropped at the fold's end (behind); a fold out of memory within 96 records keeps them, never saved. HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD live in `stats.bin` alone. S2's chunked blocks (hash → id, the rank's snapshot, the played set: 6.5) can't be resident under the budget: they are carved for a rank's or a page's life and given back (13).

**The fold round.**
- The boot's fold gives its plays round `HEAD.round + 1`; live folds this session, `HEAD.round + 2`. The save writes the highest round used.
- Live plays the next boot folds again (not saved by a rotation) get that boot's round. It is higher than the round they had in RAM: the order holds, the number differs.
- So the proof (S1) compares counts byte for byte and rounds by order.
- 24 bits: 16.7 M rounds, saturating.

**The seq rule** (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:327-328`): `seq_ = max(NVS's seq, the card log's, MARK's, ACKS's)` for this device and epoch.
- Today a settings partition written back from a backup (the house's own end-of-check step: `docs/RELEASE-0.14.0-CHECKS.md:116-118`) puts NVS's seq behind.
- On a card whose log was trimmed, new plays then reuse seqs under ACKS: they read as acknowledged and are never sent.
- They are also under MARK, so the stats would skip them.
- 2.20.4 lets a device reserve seqs, so a higher start breaks no reader. Its own commit in S1.
- Two places state today's rule and change with it: METADATA 2.20.4's "At boot" sentence (`docs/METADATA.md:3112-3122`, C2) and the reference that writes the play log's goldens, `tools/playlog.py`'s `boot_seq()` (`:244-248`, used by `Device.boot` at `:531-545`; C5).

**The play log after S1**, against 0.14.0:
- the scan reads on a new epoch with no ACKS;
- the trim also needs the folds;
- the rotation may save first;
- the seq rule as above.

Slots, files, ACKS and the transfer's reading are unchanged. `test_play_log`'s 33 tests (`test/test_play_log/test_play_log.cpp:3064-3096`) pass with no `Folds`, with `atBoot`'s count from 5 to 6 (`:454-455`: the seq rule's new golden case, C5).

### 5.7 Card events

| Event | What happens |
|---|---|
| Remount during the scan | The scan begins again (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:167-174`); the folds are dropped (the records, any overflow and the ranges' block), and `stats.bin` is read again at the scan's first piece (one read, no waits, nothing carved to keep: a MARK past the room the boot carved is the stats off, 5.4), and a `stats.bin` unread at the boot is tried again there |
| Remount after Ready, before this boot's first slot | The scan begins again (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:171-173`; `src/app/PlayHistory.h:84-87`); `stats.bin` is read again (`scanBegan(again)` drops the folds, the read at the scan's first piece, one read, no waits) and the boot's save runs again (nothing new: it writes nothing) |
| Remount after the first slot | The live folds go on; the next save checks the card's copy first (5.5), so a card that was in another player meanwhile is never saved over |
| A remount inside the orderly end's flush (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:288-290`) | No read; the folds are dropped and no save follows (5.4) |
| A log file the boot couldn't read whole (an open or a read the card failed, or an open that found the file system's records on the way to it damaged: the S1 review) | A partial scan: the fold goes on, no save this session, the trim by the MARK read at the boot; the next boot folds again (5.6) |
| A library update | Nothing of the stats (the rework, 5.14: no give-back, no save first, no read after it). The boot's scan doesn't begin while an update is asked (the plays wait in the Tracker); once it has begun, an update asked waits in Asked while the scan holds its ranges' block or overflow chunks (`LibraryUpdate::Wait::Stats`), and Auto DJ's join doesn't begin meanwhile. The records' save waits while the fence is up, until the records reach 96 (5.5). (S1 as first built gave its blocks back at the update's memory check or fence and read `stats.bin` again after it: 6.7's history) |
| The boot's save doesn't land with an overflow (past 96 records) | Tried again 100, 300 and 1,000 ms apart, four tries, the overflow held and a track's start, Auto DJ's join and an asked update waiting meanwhile; then behind: no save this session, the next boot folds what the log still holds (5.5) |
| Card pulled | No save, no trim. Plays not written were never folded |
| Another card in the slot | FatFs refuses writes (STA_PROTECT) and the guard restarts, or under the breaker it doesn't (`src/main.cpp:3199-3236`). The save checks `foreign` and `writesOff` and writes nothing. RAM is the first card's and is dropped at the restart |
| Card full | The save fails (5.5); the trim follows the old MARK. The records' save is tried again at each later record; with the records full the 97th puts the session behind (5.5) |
| Read-only (`writesOff`) | Save and trim fail; nothing lost |
| Reformatted, or `/.player` deleted | `stats.bin` is absent with the log: counting starts again (a player that stayed on through it saves nothing more this session: 5.5) |
| Only `stats.bin` deleted | Counting starts from what the log still holds; older history is gone |
| NVS erased | A new epoch, a new MARK row; the old epoch's slots fold once under their own row |

### 5.8 Every player on the card, each slot once

- The stats count every deviceId (O2). ACKS's 16 rows govern only the trim (`lib/core/CardManifest.h:159-175`).
- A card used by two players: each folds every slot above the card's MARK and saves one `stats.bin`. A player's last session, not yet saved, is folded by whichever player boots with the card next.
- Each slot once:
  - one atomic file holds the aggregate and its MARK;
  - MARK rows are kept for good (5.2);
  - the boot folds only above MARK, each slot once (5.6);
  - a scan begun again drops its folds; a partial scan saves nothing (5.6);
  - a save over a `stats.bin` that isn't the copy RAM stands on is refused (5.5);
  - live folds are RAM only, of Written slots, with fresh seqs (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:531-535`);
  - the trim and the rotation delete only what the card's MARK covers, or what is saved first;
  - a session behind its log (RAM lacking slots the log holds above the card's MARK: the records full after a save that didn't land, a boot overflow not saved, a held tmp lost, a section damaged) saves nothing more, so no MARK passes a slot not folded (5.5); a library update asks nothing of the stats (the rework);
  - seqs only rise (`docs/METADATA.md:3109-3131`; and 5.6's rule).
- Rare cases that undercount by design:
  - a slot given up on (3 card failures) whose bytes had landed, under a MARK raised later that session: never folded;
  - a firmware without stats (0.14.0) trims ACKed files it never folded (`lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:332-344`). The next stats boot sees ACKS above MARK with those seqs gone, and HEAD's gap says how many.

### 5.9 The clock and the time zone

- **Unset** (startedAt 0, clock 0: `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:692-693`): HOUR, DAYS and RCNT need a day, so an undated play counts only in HEAD (in `undated` too) and in its TRKS row (firstAt 0 only while the row has no dated play, lastAt untouched, lastOrd its round: 5.2's merge).
- **Set during a play:** the slot is dated from its start (`lib/core/WallClock.cpp:167-180`); earlier slots stay 0. Dating them back from a later slot of the same boot (2.20.6 item 7) is possible later, only if the live and the boot's folds do it alike.
- **The RTC not believed** at the boot (`wallclock::Boot::Foreign` and the rest: `lib/core/WallClock.h:101-119`): as unset.
- **Corrections:** a `@time` moves the clock either way (`src/app/BoardClock.cpp:363-366`); the RTC drifts about 3.5 s a day (`docs/RELEASE-0.14.0-CHECKS.md:59`). A later today than a play's day is fine; counting days never go under 1.
- **Another device's** startedAt outside 2026-2099 is undated: the u24 hour and u16 day would overflow or go negative.
- **UTC now (O4).** Hours and days are UTC. Every function that turns them into labels or windows (lib/core `StatsView`, `StatsText`) takes `tzOffsetMin`, 0 until S6.
- **The time zone (S6).**
  - `@time` parses only field 0 today (`lib/core/HostLink.cpp:327-339`). Extra fields are ignored (`lib/core/HostLine.h:91-98`), so 0.14.0 takes a terminal that sends more.
  - S6 reads an optional field 1, minutes east of UTC (−720 to +840), into `HostLink::Out` (`lib/core/HostLink.h:109`), keeps it in NVS, and shifts the display.
  - Nothing stored changes: HOUR gives local days for at least the last 35 days, and a change of zone (DST, travel) moves the whole display at once and misfiles nothing.

### 5.10 NVS

- No key for the stats, and no write a play (`src/app/NvsLater.h:19-21`). `plays` is untouched (`lib/core/NvsLayout.h:210-236`).
- S6's `clock/tz` (an i16) is schema 7 (`kCurrent` at `lib/core/NvsLayout.h:77`), with a `migrate(6, 7)` that does nothing (`src/app/NvsSchema.cpp:42-53`): no key reads as UTC. Older firmware never reads it.
- The Bluetooth EQ claims the next schema too (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:1866-1869`); whichever lands second takes the next number (10).

### 5.11 What the terminal may do

- It never writes `/.player` (2.11: read only). The listener may delete it whole (2.11's Listener column, `docs/METADATA.md:1790`), and a format the listener confirms (`@format`) clears it too: the stats start again with the log. For caches "the device rebuilds" holds; for the stats it doesn't, and 2.11's row says so (C2).
- It never reads or writes the stats files. `device.txt` gains no key.
- Its sync's ACKS lets the next boot trim, after the fold and the save.
- Later (not v1): it may write mStream's per-track counts as a gen-named file of its own (spec 8.6). Not played yet would then mean never, and the counts would include other clients.

### 5.12 Compatibility

`stats.bin` is primary data, not a cache. `library.idx` answers a version change with one rebuild (`Load::Outdated`); copying that for the stats would lose, for good, every play the trims already removed. So:
- a reader keeps reading every major it ever read;
- a newer major is left alone (Off);
- a minor adds sections or longer rows only for what a save by an older firmware can drop (re-derived, or counted again from the log). A section a minor adds is written OPTIONAL (flags without `kSectionRequired`, `lib/core/CardContainer.h:42`), so an older reader skips it instead of reading the file as UnknownRequired and turning the stats Off; only major 1's own sections are REQUIRED (5.2). Anything that must not be lost needs a new major.

| Combination | Result |
|---|---|
| 0.14.0 on a card with `stats.bin` | Never reads it. Trims ACKed files without folding them: those plays are missed when the card comes back. HEAD's gap counts them |
| Stats firmware after a 0.14.0 session | Folds what the log still holds above MARK |
| A newer major on the card | Stats Off this session; no trim; the log rotates as ever |
| A newer minor | Read by its known sections and row prefixes (its added sections are OPTIONAL, so they are skipped); a save writes this minor (drops the additions: the rule above) |
| `stats.bin` corrupt | `stats.bad`; counting starts again from the log |
| The base lost or wrong | Per-track rows before it lost (shelves thinner); totals intact; flags bit 1 |
| `/.player` deleted | Starts again |
| Two players alternating, either firmware with stats | Each slot once (5.8) |
| A unit alternating 0.14.0 and stats firmware | Undercounts what 0.14.0 trims; no double count |

### 5.13 A worked example

Device A, epoch E. `stats.bin` says MARK (A, E) = 41. `plays.1.jnl` holds seqs 30-45, `plays.jnl` 46-48. ACKS (A, E) = 45.

| Seq | Slot | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 42 | Track X, durationMs 200,000, playedMs 120,000, completed, Auto DJ, startedAt 2026-11-08T21:40:00Z | Play (line 30,000). Day 311, hour 7,485, time 26,948,401 |
| 43 | Track Y, durationMs 240,000, playedMs 12,000, skipped | Skip (line 30,000) |
| 44 | Track Y, durationMs 40,000, playedMs 25,000, stopped | Play (line 20,000) |
| 45 | durationMs 0, playedMs 8,000, stopped | Neither (line 30,000): 8 s listened |
| 46-48 | Three plays | Plays |
| 30-41 | | Under MARK: not folded |

- Day: January to October 2026 are 304 days, so 8 November is day 311 (2026-01-01 is 0). Hour: 311 x 24 + 21. Time: 311 x 86,400 + 21 x 3,600 + 40 x 60 + 1.
- Y's row: plays 1, skips 1, firstAt and lastAt 44's time.
- MARK becomes 48 and the save writes it.
- The trim: `plays.1.jnl` (30-45) is under the new MARK and under ACKS: it goes. `plays.jnl` (46-48) is under MARK but over ACKS: it stays.
- The next boot finds nothing above 48: no save.

### 5.14 S1 rework: a fixed resident set (2026-11-12)

**Designed and reviewed; R1 built (the yardstick as a test, `test_autodj_join`'s `test_the_listening_stats_across_updates`, failing on purpose against S1 as built until R6, passing since), R2 built (the reference and its 34 sessions; `test_stats_store` fails two cases on purpose until R5), R3 built (`lib/core/StatsFold`, the rework's fold; S1 as built's moved to `StatsFoldS1` until R6), R4 built (`lib/core/StatsFile`, the streaming read and save; S1 as built's store moved to `StatsFileS1` until R6), R5 built (the glue and the firmware: `lib/core/StatsGlue`, `app/ListeningStats`, `PlayHistory`, `main.cpp`, `LibraryUpdate`'s `Wait::Stats`; `test_stats_store` runs every session through the glue), R6 built (the heap model: `test_autodj_join`'s `StatsRun` runs the firmware's glue, and the yardstick passes; S1 as built's fold and store deleted), R7 built (the docs: METADATA 3.11, ARCHITECTURE, the README's `H`, and this plan's 1, 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11, 12 and 13 describing the rework as built); the rework review applied (2026-11-16: its 17 findings, the build plan's "The rework review"); its gate met on the host (2026-11-16, at 39513f1: section 1's "The rework's gate"); the device re-run made (2026-11-17: main's PSRAM figures at every point but the FLAC row), and the resident set moved to internal RAM for it, 40 records (2026-11-17: "Moved to internal RAM", below); that move's re-run owed.** The owner's decision of 2026-11-12, "Rework S1": the stats keep a small fixed amount in memory, set aside once at the boot and never moved, and merge the rest from the card at each save. With nothing big resident, the give-back around library updates goes. Since R7, 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11 and 13 describe the rework as built (until then they described S1 as built, 414488c), and this section keeps the design, its review and each step's "As built"; where the code and this section disagree, the code wins and the step's "As built" says so. The design's review (2026-11-12, two lenses: the data's integrity and the memory) kept its core and its one resident block: every merge is ⊕, associative and order-free with saturation; HOUR's, DAYS's and RCNT's cuts give the same rows whether the card's copy was cut already or not (rows only grow, and statsref's `retain()` prunes in place); MARK never passes a slot not folded; the trim and the rotation go by the card's MARK. What it found wrong or missing is folded into the rules below, and each finding's outcome is listed at the end ("The review's findings").

**What the device found** (S1's device round 2 and main's control, 2026-11-12; MEASURED on the same board and spare card with one script: album 966, `gb` with 25.8 s of the heard track left, `D` and `Dc` after each update; logs in the session's scratch folder, `dev/round2` and `dev/control`). PSRAM's largest free block:

| Point | main (218822a) | S1 (414488c) |
|---|---|---|
| The boot's join, a build at the boot | 573,428 B | 516,084 B (-57,344) |
| Before any update (the Opus's 128 KB kept since run H) | 442,356 B | 385,012 B (-57,344) |
| After the MP3 update | 294,900 B | 294,900 B |
| After the Opus update (the fence at once, track 07 opening behind it: main's run Lo2) | 294,900 B | 188,404 B (-106,496: under 245,764 B where main is over it) |
| After the FLAC update (the FLAC held through it) | 188,404 B | 120,820 B (-67,584) |

The comparison found the mechanism and reproduced it in the heap model: S1's resident blocks (62,296 B on this card), carved in setup and carved again by good fit into the holes each rejoin leaves (the take-back's re-read), move where the long-lived blocks carved between updates land: the Opus decoder's, kept from its first open, and a FLAC's while it plays. The give-back frees the stats' blocks, not the ones they moved. E's deferred update (15,148 B short) is most likely the same cost from the boot (an inference: main can't repeat it without the card's earlier state).

**The yardstick: the multi-update model** (MEASURED-HOST, 2026-11-12; re-run after the review). The comparison's harness: `test_a_library_update_in_a_model_heap`'s model heap with 5 update steps of the 19,412-track library, the decoders' long-lived carves, a `Dc` (the join again) after each step or not, no memory-check give-back (run L had none), joined measured per step against main's run in the same history. The rule at each step is the mood plan's: joined at or over 245,764 B where main's is, and within 122,880 B of main's.
- **The decoders' carves are the device's now** (the review). The harness had asked for the Opus's carved sizes, 65,536 and 62,464 B, which `ModelHeap::carved()` rounds again (67,584 and 63,488 B), and for a FLAC one 65,536 B block and 16,384 B. The device asks for 65,307, 61,440 and 1,276 B for the Opus (`src/audio/OpusGenerator.cpp:50-52`; the logs' `[opus] buffers: 128023 B`), which carve 65,536, 62,464 and 1,312 B; and libFLAC (ESP8266Audio's, built with `FLAC__ALIGN_MALLOC_DATA` and the 64 Kbit bit reader of a non-ESP8266 build) for its private struct (about 6 KB), an 8,192 B bit reader (`bitreader.c:106, 274`), then each channel's output and residual, 16,400 and 16,415 B (`stream_decoder.c:1316-1325`): no block over about 16.9 KB, 82,072 B with headers in the model against the 82,120 B the control measured (`mainrun.log`: 429,340 B free while the FLAC played, 511,460 B after `qx`).
- **Both shapes are run**: the device's ("dev", the measure from here on) and the old one ("old", what the design was first drawn on), each over the wide set (every combination of the Opus kept never, before step 0, or after step 0-3; a FLAC held through no step or one of steps 0-4; a `Dc` after each step or not: 72 histories, 360 steps) and its named subsets: H1-H4 (the comparison's), H5-H8, K1-K4 and D (round 2's own sequence, below). The harness is `rework/r2/m.cpp` in the session's scratch folder (the review's copy with both shapes, the FatFiles and the shapes below), built with the host's g++ 11.2.0 against the worktree's native `libcore.a` (from 23:04, before f073406; it touches only `Fold` and `sizeof(Glue)`, as in the comparison); its outputs are `rework/r2/out*.txt`.

| History, step (dev) | main | S1 as built (P = 2,214, 300 slots, the give-back) | The rework (below) |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 plain (no decoder, no `Dc`), steps 0-4 | 360,436 B each | 360,436 B each | 360,436 B each |
| H2 (the Opus kept before step 0, a FLAC through step 3, `Dc`), 0-4 | 249,844, 249,844, 360,436, 180,212, 270,324 B | **180,212 B (-69,632): fails**, 249,844, 249,844 (-110,592), 180,212, 249,844 B (-20,480) | 249,844, 249,844, 360,436, 112,628 (-67,584: main is under the line there), 270,324 B |
| H3 (the Opus kept after step 2, `Dc`), 0-4 | 360,436, 360,436, 360,436, 270,324, 249,844 B | 360,436, 360,436, 294,900 (-65,536), **159,732 (-110,592)**, **159,732 B (-90,112)**: both fail | main's at every step |
| H4 (the Opus kept after step 0, `Dc`), 0-4 | 294,900, 172,020, 172,020, 106,484, 172,020 B | 294,900, 159,732 (-12,288), 159,732 (-12,288), 159,732 (+53,248), 270,324 B (+98,304) | main's at every step |

| The boot's join | 589,812 B | 507,892 B (-81,920) | 589,812 B |

| Set: mean against main, worst, steps failing | dev: S1 as built | dev: the rework | dev: main's queue 2,048 B longer | old: S1 as built | old: the rework | old: main's queue 2,048 B longer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1-H4 (20 steps) | -16,998 B, -110,592 B, 3 | -3,379 B, -67,584 B, 0 | +0 B, +0 B, 0 | -28,877 B, -118,784 B, 3 | -205 B, -4,096 B, 0 | +5,325 B, -4,096 B, 0 |
| H5-H8 (20) | -31,334 B, -110,592 B, 2 | -5,734 B, -110,592 B, 0 | -205 B, -4,096 B, 0 | -5,120 B, -110,592 B, 1 | +0 B, +0 B, 0 | +10,854 B, +0 B, 0 |
| K1-K4 (20) | -30,720 B, -110,592 B, 2 | -614 B, -61,440 B, 0 | -3,072 B, -61,440 B, 0 | -12,083 B, -110,592 B, 2 | -3,891 B, -86,016 B, 2 | +0 B, -81,920 B, 2 |
| D (5) | -22,938 B, -69,632 B, 1 | -16,384 B, -61,440 B, 0 | -12,288 B, -61,440 B, 0 | -20,480 B, -155,648 B, 1 | +18,022 B, -20,480 B, 0 | +18,022 B, -20,480 B, 0 |
| The wide set (360) | -21,959 B, -180,224 B, **47** | -1,246 B, -110,592 B, **0** | -1,564 B, -110,592 B, 2 | -17,095 B, -200,704 B, **36** | -603 B, -90,112 B, **2** (both among the control's) | +4,369 B, -94,208 B, 6 |

"The rework" in these tables is the design below: the block of 3,584 B at `PlayHistory::begin()`'s place; a records' save (its work memory and two FatFiles) carved and freed after each step's `Dc` and in each fence's pass; at the boot, the scan first, holding the ranges' block (3,072 B) at the top keeping 64 KB free, the Opus opening while it is held where the history opens it before step 0, then the boot's save (its work memory and FatFiles in one call) and the boot's join. Its wide-set figures equal those of the 3,584 B block alone with the Opus opening before the boot's join (the same order), in both shapes; with the old shape, those of the block alone. H5's step 4 (-110,592 B in dev) is main's own noise: 249,844 B, over the line, and any held block from 2,048 B on gives the same there.

**The model doesn't reproduce round 2** (the review). Round 2's sequence is history D: the Opus kept before the first update, the MP3 update, the Opus update, the FLAC playing through the third, a `Dc` after each. On the device S1 minus main was 0, -106,496 and -67,584 B at those three updates, main 294,900, 294,900 and 188,404 B. The model on D: 0, +36,864 and -155,648 B in the old shape (main 249,844, 249,844, 294,900 B); -69,632, 0 and +36,864 B in the device's (main 249,844, 249,844, 180,212 B). The design had matched round 2's steps against H2's steps 0, 2 and 3, a history with the FLAC through the fourth update: a fit chosen after the fact, now withdrawn. So R1 shows the rework within main's own variation in the model, not what the device will show: **the device re-run against main's figures, not R1, accepts the block's size and the transients' placement.**

**The wide set, and what it says about any fixed set.** One block carved at `PlayHistory::begin()`'s place and held (MEASURED-HOST):

| Held from setup | dev: mean, worst, failing of 360 | old: mean, worst, failing of 360 |
|---|---|---|
| 512 B | +0 B, +0 B, 0 | +0 B, +0 B, 0 |
| 1,024 B | -1,468 B, -110,592 B, 1 | -216 B, -77,824 B, 0 |
| 2,048 B | -1,360 B, -110,592 B, 0 | -762 B, -90,112 B, 2 |
| 3,072 B | -950 B, -110,592 B, 0 | -364 B, -90,112 B, 2 |
| **3,584 B, the rework's block** (with its transients: the same) | **-933 B, -110,592 B, 0** | **-603 B, -90,112 B, 2** (both among the control's 6) |
| 4,096 B | -933 B, -110,592 B, 0 | -1,041 B, -118,784 B, 4 |
| 6,144 B | -5,308 B, -110,592 B, 0 | -1,667 B, -118,784 B, 4 |
| 8,192 B | -4,483 B, -110,592 B, 3 | -2,406 B, -122,880 B, 21 |
| 12,288 B | -4,278 B, -126,976 B, 11 | -5,882 B, -126,976 B, 31 |
| **main's queue block 2,048 B longer** (main's own variation, the control) | -1,564 B, -110,592 B, **2** | +4,369 B, -94,208 B, **6** |
| S1 as built (62-79 KB, given back and taken again) | -21,959 B, -180,224 B, 47 | -17,095 B, -200,704 B, 36 |

- **The place doesn't matter; the bytes do** (old shape, the design's run). 37,344 B gives the same figures carved first in setup, at `begin()`, at setup's end, after the boot's join, at the top of PSRAM, as one block or seven (H1-H4: -46,899 B mean, 7 of 20 failing). In setup every carve comes from the boot's one free block, so a block anywhere in it moves everything carved after it by its bytes. No placement makes a resident set free.
- **The cost is not a clean staircase, and its edges move with the decoders' shapes** (the review). In the old shape 4,096 B lost two steps more than 3,584 B (K1 and K2: a FLAC's one 64 KB block held through step 4), which was the design's reason for 3,584 B; in the device's shape 4,096 B equals 3,584 B, nothing from 2,048 to 6,144 B fails a step, and 1,024 B fails one that 2,048 B doesn't. What both shapes agree on: up to about 4 KB a set fails only steps main's own variation fails too, and from 8 KB it fails steps the control doesn't (old 21, dev 3; 12 KB: 31 and 11). So **3,584 B is a budget**, held by the `static_assert` and confirmed by the device re-run, not an edge the model found: it leaves room under the 4-6 KB the model can't tell from main's noise, and the rule for S2-S6 (nothing added to the block unless R1 still passes) rests on the corrected model.
- **A block carved and freed in one call leaves no trace.** A records' save (its work memory and two FatFiles, 19,192 B in three blocks) after each step's `Dc` and in each fence's pass, the boot's save's work memory and FatFiles, and a records' save at 96 in update 0's fence, before or after its build (the review's case, below): every figure equals the block's alone, in both shapes.
- **The update's wait for the scan is load-bearing** (the review). With the scan's blocks held through update 0's build, or carved in its fence and freed after its build (what dropping `mayScan()`'s wait for an asked update would allow), steps main passes fail: a full log's overflow held through the build 34 of 360 in both shapes (in the old one two FLAC carves fail besides); carved in the fence 48 (old) and 35 (dev); even the usual boot's work memory alone held through the build 35 and 15. R1's case runs the first two with the design's own scan blocks (the ranges' 3,072 B and 16 chunks, the boot's save's work memory and three FatFiles carved and freed at the scan's end; the runs above held the work memory with the chunks): held through the build 34 of 360 with the device's decoders and 24 with the old (two decoder blocks finding no room besides), carved in the fence 25 and 48 (MEASURED-HOST). Freed before the first update's memory check, as the waits below make it, they cost nothing more than the cases that follow. (As built, R6, with the firmware's blocks: held through the build, 13 of 65 steps fail and 62 of 360 (60 beyond the control's) with the device's decoders, 9 of 65 (7 beyond) and 31 of 360 (29) with the old; carved in the fence, 1 and 25, 12 (10) and 48 (45). MEASURED-HOST.)
- **A decoder opening under the boot's held blocks** (a tap on Play during the boot's scan; re-run in both shapes, wide set, steps failing of 360, old / dev). Main with the Opus opening before the boot's join fails none (old 0 / dev 0), and the 3,584 B block with that order 2 / 0, so the order alone costs nothing:

| Held across the scan's passes while the Opus opens | The join first, then the scan | The scan first, the join waiting |
|---|---|---|
| **The rework: the ranges' block (3,072 B), at the top keeping 64 KB** | 2 / 0 | **2 / 0** |
| The ranges' block by good fit | | 2 / 0 |
| The design before the review: the scan's work memory (10,896 B), at the top keeping 64 KB | 2 / 0 | 2 / **8** |
| ... at the top keeping nothing | | 2 / 0 |
| ... by good fit | 3 / **8** | 3 / 0 |
| A full log's overflow with the ranges (3,072 B and 16 chunks of 16,384 B), at the top keeping 64 KB | 2 / 0 | 2 / **29** (all 12 histories with the Opus before step 0 fail steps 0 and 1: 180,212 B against 249,844 B) |
| ... with the work memory (10,896 B and 16 chunks), at the top keeping 64 KB | 2 / **10** | 2 / 1 |
| ... keeping nothing | 2 / 3 | 2 / 5 |
| ... by good fit | 1 / 3 | 3 / 4 |
| **... the Opus opening only after it is given back (a track's start waiting: the rework)** | 2 / 0 (the work memory's) | **2 / 0** (the ranges') |

  In the old shape none of these fails a step the block alone doesn't (the design's finding). In the device's the scan's 10,896 B held across passes failed 8 in two placements of five, and the ranges' 3,072 B none in any: so the scan holds only its ranges (below), and the boot's save carves its work memory in its own call. A full log's overflow (about 270 KB) is another matter: with the Opus opening while it is held it fails 0 to 29 steps in the device's shape, by the order of the scan and the join and by a few KB of the scan's first block, and no placement tried makes it free; with the Opus opening after it, none. So **a track's start waits while the boot's fold holds overflow chunks** (below): about 1.4 s and its save for a full log, once a card (the first stats boot on a card 0.14.0 never synced, or the first after a 0.14.0 player's long use). The top keeping 64 KB stays (the review's reason; good fit is no better). Run I on the device measures the overflow with a tap on Play during it. (As built, R6, MEASURED-HOST, the firmware's blocks: the Opus opening under the scan's blocks, a full log's (the ranges' 1,284 B and 16 chunks) or a usual boot's (the ranges and the one chunk its first piece reserves), the scan first, fails no step beyond the control's in either shape, named or wide; with the device's decoders its boot's join is main's with the Opus opening before the boot's join (466,932 B: the order's own cost, against 589,812 B after it), with the old shapes 16,384 B under that (450,548 B, in each of the 12 histories of 72 with the Opus at the boot). The raw blocks of the table had failed 29: the ranges' 3,072 B, and the boot's save's blocks carved while all the scan's were held, where the glue gives the ranges' block back first and the chunks while its work memory is held. So with the device's decoders the start's wait is not load-bearing in the model; it is kept, the owner's default.) (The rework review, MEASURED-HOST: R6 had measured it with the ranges' block at 1,284 B alone and the Opus opening after the scan's last piece. Opening after the full log's first piece or its 50th gives the same figures; but with the root's ACKS 16 rows above MARK, the ranges' block at its largest (3,076 B, a card a 0.14.0 player synced before its first stats boot), the Opus opening under a full log's overflow fails 10 of 65 steps (H2, H5, K3, K4 and D at steps 0 and 1: 180,212 B against main's 249,844 B) and the boot's join 401,396 B against main's with the Opus first 466,932 B; 29 of 360 in the wide set (mean -9,455 B, worst -180,224 B), and with the old shapes none beyond the control's. So the start's wait is load-bearing at the ranges' block's largest, and the rework with it (that full log, a start waiting) fails none.)
- **The boot's join must not place around a held overflow**: with a full log's overflow (the work memory and 16 chunks, 281 KB) held and the Opus open, the join can't place in 12 of the 72 histories (all 12 with the Opus kept before step 0) and the boot's joined is 155,636 B against 589,812 B (both shapes). So the join waits while the scan's blocks are held, and the hold is short (below).

**Moved to internal RAM (2026-11-17; the owner's decision, "Move it").** The rework's device re-run (2026-11-17, the spare card, the control's script with main's control in the same sequence; logs `dev/rework` and `dev/control2`) matched main's PSRAM figures exactly at every point but one: after the library update made while a FLAC plays, the largest free block was 106,484 B against main's 188,404 B, the same again in a second run. The one standing PSRAM difference was free 3,268-3,276 B lower: the glue's object (3,200 B and its heap header). It shifts the holes, so Auto DJ's 76,944 B kept block no longer fits its usual one ("2 of 3 kept" where main keeps 3 of 3). The heap model had predicted no cost for that history (D), so only the device judges PSRAM, and the owner chose to take the stats out of it:
- **The object is internal DRAM's `.bss`** (`app/ListeningStats.cpp`'s `s_glue`, made with placement new in `ListeningStats::begin()`, never destroyed), not a carve from the internal heap. It is never in the heap, so it never splits a free block another internal carve would have taken (the card worker's 7.5 KB stack at each burst of jobs, A2DP's buffers, a track's 4.1 KB of decoder buffers), as a carve in setup would, and it can't fail. It does shrink the DRAM heap above `.bss` by its size (the internal-RAM review): that heap runs from `_heap_start`, `.dram0.bss`'s end, to 0x3FFE0000 (79,288 B in the review's build, 2,096 B less than S0's), and `heap_caps_malloc()` tries the internal heaps in address order, DRAM's before the D/IRAM ones above it, so a carve that fit at that heap's end on main can go to the next internal heap instead. No free block is split, but carves can shift between heaps (where Bluedroid's link buffers, the card worker's stack and a track's decoder buffers land), the same class of effect as the FLAC row in PSRAM, which `ram=` and `min=` alone wouldn't show, so the re-run recorded the internal `largest=` and the card worker's lowest beside main's: on the speaker one `[heap]` `largest=` moved by more than the object's 2 KB, boot 2's at `library` and `dance` (63K against main's 67K, equal again at `ui`), and one `[rate]` line's largest block (`lo2`'s Opus table copy, 47,092 B against main's 51,188 B; the other `[rate]` lines 2,048 B under), and nothing failed; the worker's lowest while a step ran (a sampled figure) moved by 1,912-2,320 B with the headphones linked (below, "Its device re-run"). Its cost is the same at every boot, card or none, and shows in the build's RAM figure (`.dram0.bss` +1,992 B; 2,096 B over S0's with the rework's 96 B of statics and the review's 8 B, `Ht`'s hash).
- **MARK's block stays in PSRAM, by plain `psramAlloc` since fix G** (2026-11-17, below; from the internal-RAM review until then at the top of its largest free block through `topAlloc()`, which would have held a free block T above it for the session: B1; the move had put it on the internal heap). A card with more than 8 MARK rows gets it: its rows and 8 more, 28 B a row (476 B at 9 rows, 560 B at 12), through `statsglue::Memory::keep`, the firmware's `psramAlloc` and `psramFree`, carved once by the boot's read in setup and held for the session. Its size is the card's, with no bound (MARK's rows are never dropped: each (deviceId, epoch) folded, so each player and each NVS erase, adds one), and internal RAM's margin has none to give: on the internal heap a 9-row card's block (476 B and its header) would have put the walk under G5's 42 KB at METADATA 3.8's figures (the budget bullet), and a card of hundreds of rows would have taken tens of KB in setup, after the audio's stage, before the A2DP link's buffers, the card worker's stack and the decoders' buffers. So internal RAM holds the object alone, whatever the card. A card of 8 rows or fewer (the spare card has 2) carves none; a card past 8 rows holds that block for the session where the heap puts it (above the read's work memory and FatFile unless a hole fits it), main having no such block. With no PSRAM for it the read is `NoMemory`, and the stats are off. (Internal instead is the owner's call, and needs a cap counted in the budget, `static_assert(sizeof(statsglue::Glue) + cap + 16 <= 2048)`: 40 B, under the smallest block (9 rows and 8 more: 476 B), so the object would have to shrink first.)
- **Nothing of the stats is held in PSRAM between calls but the boot's scan's blocks** (and MARK's block on a card past 8 rows, above). **Not "so PSRAM is main's"** (B1, below): the blocks held across passes left a free block T above them for the hold, and a full log's chunks gaps between them, where a long-lived carve made meanwhile (the first frame's canvas, a queue) pinned the largest free block split for the session; fix A closes T with caps and puts the overflow in one arena. The ranges' block (1,284-3,076 B) is given back at the fold's end: the boot's save, `ready()` after a partial scan, `scanAway()`, or a remount's `scanBegan(true)`. A fold past the records holds overflow chunks until the boot's save lands or is held; after `[plays] ready`, only while that save is tried again (100, 300 and 1,000 ms apart), then behind. Every other PSRAM block the stats carve is given back in its call: each read's, save's and look's work memory (`statsfile::kWorkBytes`, 11,456 B), the FatFiles of the files it opens (`FatFs::open()`'s `psramNew<FatFile>`, 4,148 B) and the FILINFOs of its questions (`FatFs::look()`, `stat()`). **The audit** (2026-11-17), every PSRAM call of the stats' code:
  - `app/ListeningStats.cpp`: `psramLargest()` and `psramFreeBytes()` (questions, for `topAlloc()`'s spacers); `topAlloc()` (its spacers given back in the call; then the ranges' block and the overflow's arena, the scan's); `Memory::alloc` and `release` (`psramAlloc`, `psramFree`: the work memory, in `Glue::readNow()`, `commit()`, `track()`, `lookFile()` and `lookDays()`'s `lookRows()`, given back in each). `Memory::keep` is plain `psramAlloc` since fix G (MARK's block, held for the session where the heap puts it). Since fix A, `topAlloc()` and `topRelease()` are an `autodj::TopHold`: the spacers, the block (the ranges' block, the overflow's arena; no room for the arena, its chunks one by one, carved as before the caps), then `heap_caps_walk(MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM)` (a question under the heap's lock) and the caps (`psramAlloc`, held with the block and freed after it, bottom-up; given back early, `TopHold::uncap()`, when the overflow falls back to chunks); the boot's first take also asks `heap_caps_get_info()`, and a take no free block can hold asks `heap_caps_get_largest_free_block()` and carves nothing.
  - `lib/core`'s `StatsFold`, `StatsFile` and `StatsGlue` carve only through those hooks: `Fold::carveChunk()` by `top`, the store's MARK block by `keep`, the rest placement new into the work memory. Their default hooks (`malloc`) are the host's.
  - `PlayHistory`'s own PSRAM (the read buffer, the Tracker, the Journal, the Outbox) is the play log's, main's since 0.14.0.
  - The console's looks that read the card (`H`'s card part, `Hd`, `Ht`) carve a save's work memory and a FatFile, but as moved they ran at once on the loop, at no pass the write gate chose: typed in a start's, a jump's or a join's window, their blocks could be held while the decode task carves a track's long-lived blocks (the first Opus's 128,023 B of buffers kept for the boot, a FLAC's six blocks for its track), and PSRAM would differ from main's for the session or the track (the internal-RAM review). Since the review they wait as `Hs` does: `command()` notes the look (`H`'s RAM part, `Hm`, and a reply that needs no card print at once), and `PlayHistory::loop()` makes it in `Hs`'s turn (`turnOf(saveDue, slotsDue, hsWaiting || lookWaiting)`, `Hs` first), one a pass, at a pass the write gate lets through after a save due and the slots due, and never behind the library update's fence (no save carves there either). Their depth from `PlayHistory::loop` (`-fstack-usage`, FatFs's own frames not counted): `Hd`'s open 1,296 B, its line with printf's own about 2,096 B; `Ht`'s open 1,248 B; `H`'s card part's open 848 B (each look's lines, printf's own with them, under `Hd`'s); all under the boot's save's line (2,208 B) and the rotation's save (2,464 B).
- **The budget is internal RAM's margin** (11's Internal RAM row): **2,048 B**, the `static_assert` on `sizeof(statsglue::Glue)` (the PSRAM budget was 3,584 B), set against G5's floors (docs/OPUS.md: steady 50 KB or more, the session's lowest 42 KB or more, the headphones linked) at the lowest figures then held with a link (METADATA 3.8's check, 2026-11-04, on another firmware). At those figures 3,200 B would have put the walk and the steady figure under (42,268 B; 50,048 B). What the device loses is the build's `.dram0.bss` over main's, not the object alone (the internal-RAM review: as moved, this bullet counted 2,048 B and said it kept G5's floors): 2,096 B over S0's (the object's 1,992 B, the rework's 96 B of statics, the review's 8 B), and `ram=` truncates to the KB (`internalFree / 1024`). So, from those figures, `ram=52K` with a link and the worker gone (53,248-54,271 B) was expected at 51,152-52,175 B: G5's steady 50 KB (51,200 B) with 0-1 KB to spare, 48 B short at the reading's low end. A walk on the card worker under a Bluetooth play, 45,468 B, at 43,372 B, 364 B over 42 KB (43,008 B). A whole Rescan's scan under a Bluetooth play (`min=43K`, 44,032-45,055 B, that check's lowest) at 41,936-42,959 B, under 42 KB: the session's floor not shown kept. **Measured** with the headphones linked (the re-run, below, main's firmware beside the move): steady `ram=` 56K (57,344-58,367 B; main 58K), the walk's lowest 49,288 B (main 51,304 B), the Rescan's 49,124 B (main 51,000 B): both floors hold with about 6 KB to spare, on main too. The expectation was 5,169-7,215 B low, in its base, not its cost: main reads about 6 KB over 3.8's figures now, and the move costs 1,860-2,192 B against main at every figure read to the byte but one sampled (2,320 B). So G5's session floor is not the owner's call after all.
- **So the object holds 40 records, not 96** (`statsfold::kRecords`; `tools/statsref.py`'s `RECORDS`), **and their save is due at 24** (`kSaveAt`, `SAVE_AT`). The design's 2:3 is kept roughly: 16 records of slack for a fence, a backlog or a card that fails, where there were 32. The cost: `stats.bin` is written each 24 plays (about 1.5 h of music) instead of each 64, and a boot whose log holds more than 40 slots above the card's MARK overflows into a chunk (a 0.14.0 card's first stats boot, as before, or a session whose saves failed). Hm keeps 4 log keys, not 8. The object is 2,008 B on the device since fix A (`xtensa-esp32-elf-g++`'s `sizeof`; 1,992 B as moved): the Fold 1,544 B (HEAD 112, MARK's 16 rows 448, the 40 records 800, its state 184, the arena's pointer in it), the store 56, two `Opened` 112, the last save's report 60, Hm's 4 keys 96, the save's ask 32, `Memory` 24 (with `keep` and `topRelease`), the counters and flags; 40 B under its budget. Every rule of this section stands; only the counts move. Where 5.4-5.8, 6.7, 11-13 and this section's design and "As built" notes say 96 records, a records' save each 64 plays, the 97th, or a block of 3,584 B (3,200 B) in PSRAM, read 40, 24, the 41st, and an object of 2,048 B (2,008 B since fix A, 1,992 B as moved) in internal RAM. The figures they quote are their step's.

The yardstick in the suite's build: the rework fails none of 65 steps, and its boot's join is main's.   Against what was expected (the budget bullet's figures, from METADATA 3.8's of 2026-11-04 less 2,096 B): steady 51,152-52,175 B, measured 57,344-58,367 B; the walk 43,372 B, measured 49,288 B; the Rescan 41,936-42,959 B, under 42 KB, measured 49,124 B. After a flash or an upload the headphones didn't answer the next boot's three pages, twice (main's first boot, the move's first); a reset one to three minutes later linked (main's at its second page, about 14 s into the paging; the move's at its first).
- **The internal-RAM review** (2026-11-17, on f64c3f7: the internal heap, its budget, PSRAM's byte-for-byte rule). Five findings, each checked against the code, the link map and the device's logs, and fixed (the bullets above amended):
  1. and 5. MARK's block on the internal heap, its size the card's with no bound, outside the 2,048 B budget: a 9-row card would have put the walk under 42 KB at METADATA 3.8's figures. Fixed the first way the review gave: `Memory::keep` is `topAlloc()` (PSRAM's top); `StatsRun` carves it in the model heap again (a new assertion on `markHeld`, failing first with the block outside the model). The other way, internal with a cap inside the budget, leaves 40 B, no block's room: the owner's call.
  2. The budget counted the object alone, and the re-run measured no link: the budget bullet and 11's row recounted with the build's statics and `ram=`'s truncation; the headphones' runs added to the re-run; the comments (`app/ListeningStats.cpp`'s `static_assert`) reworded.
  3. "It splits no free block" said more than it can: reworded (it shrinks the DRAM heap, which can move a carve to the next internal heap); the internal `largest=` and the worker's lowest added to the re-run.
  4. The console's looks carved PSRAM at once on the loop: deferred to `Hs`'s turn (the audit, above). The deferral costs 8 B of `.bss` (`Ht`'s hash).

**The hold's T (B1, 2026-11-17) and its fix, A + G** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-17: "Fix it first", fix A and G of the investigation; built and host-gated, its device re-run owed: no device figure below is the fix's).
- **What the device found** (B1: the move's c3f1f09 on the spare card, 3 MARK rows, main's runs on the same board as the control; MEASURED, checked by an adversarial verdict against the raw logs). Free PSRAM was main's to the byte at every point, but the largest free block was split on the boots whose first frame (`[ui] up`) or a queue started from the console came inside the boot's hold. The largest free block 3 s after Auto DJ's join, an MP3 playing: main 638,964 B; the move **589,812 B** when the first frame and a start came before the boot's fold ended (I-3m and I-3m2, the same to the byte, though one folded 2 slots and wrote and the other folded none: 49,152 B, three 16 KB classes, under main's at the join; after `qx` 483,316 B against 524,276 B, 40,960 B under), and **442,356 B** after a full-log boot (I-1: 196,608 B, twelve classes, counted against main's I-3 and I-0 runs, not a main run on the same full log with `qp966`: the verdict's open question 3). The boots whose fold ended before the first frame (I-2, idle1-5) had `D`'s free and largest main's to the byte (659,348/638,964 B); their join kept 4,992 B higher than main I-0's (`free at least` 4,992 B lower), 4,784 B against main I-3's, whose joins overlapped main's own scan where the move's waits for the stats' hold (not a stats effect). So the move failed this section's acceptance (one class) on those boots. Nothing failed outright (no alarm, no failed allocation, no underrun); the cost is margin, for the session: a growing library update after an I-1-like boot is deferred to the next boot (`LibraryUpdate::roomToBuild()` wants 17/16 of the table, about 660 KB at 19.4k tracks), after an I-3m-like one it passes with about 48 KB less (about 22.2k tracks at most, about 23.6k on main).
- **Why** (the verdict's mechanism). `topAlloc()` carved a held block under Auto DJ's spacers keeping 64 KB free: the block at the low end of R, what the spacers left of the largest free block (about 66.5 KB at the boot), then the spacers given back. That left a free block T of about 57-59 KB physically above the held block, at PSRAM's top. TLSF's good fit sends any carve between about 1.3 KB and T's size made during the hold into T: the first frame's cover canvas (`NowPlayingPage`: a 352 B `M5Canvas` object and its 19,208 B sprite, carved 19,456 B, never freed), a queue (`QueueModel`, about 2.3 KB with the undo). When the hold ended the freed block merged downward, not past that pin. On a full log each of the 16 chunks was carved the same way, with gaps of 1-17 KB between them. `pmin=63K` was that window, not only the spacers. MARK's block, held for the session through `topAlloc()` since the internal-RAM review, would have left a T for the whole session on a card past 8 rows (never run: the spare card has 3 rows).
- **Fix A: close T** (`lib/core/AutoDjJoin`: `capRequest()`, `closeAbove()`, `autodj::TopHold`; `app/ListeningStats.cpp`: `topAlloc()`, `topRelease()`, the heap walk; `statsglue::Memory::topRelease`):
  - The spacers keep the margin **with the block carved**: `takeSpacers(bytes, 64 KB + carvedAtMost(bytes))`, as the join counts its own blocks. With 64 KB alone the arena's 262 KB, carved under its spacer, would have taken free PSRAM to about 16-29 KB for that instant (the spec's challengers' model); now the lowest free during a take is 67-78 KB in the model (`test_the_caps_keep_the_margin`).
  - The block, then the spacers given back, then **a heap walk** (`heap_caps_walk(MALLOC_CAP_SPIRAM)`: ESP-IDF 5.5's TLSF, light poisoning; the walker reports a block by its TLSF pointer, malloc's less the poisoning's 8 B, and its TLSF block size, the 12 B of poisoning and the class rounding in) finds the free block right above it, and **caps close it**: each the size class floor of what is left less 12 B, so that TLSF maps it to that block's own free list, whose head that block is, and carves the floor from it; each must come back at the address the walk gave (anything else, another task's carve first or another block heading the list, is freed at once: Missed, no retry, the rest left open); a walk after each. Three caps at most close any free block to 4 MB, and what is left is 0 B or a 12 B block no request can take (`capcheck.py`; `test_caps_close_any_free_block` builds every free size from 12 B to 64 KB exactly and 2,000 to 1 MB, its counts capcheck's).
  - Caps only when the block sits on the spacers' top (the free block right under it holds the last spacer's address): a block a smaller free block took (a hole) gets none, so the hole's rest stays where main's carves go. Never under the margin (64 KB and 1 KB) free: checked against the first walk's free block, and again before every cap after the first, which also stops when the block above is larger than T less the caps' footprints (another task freed the block right above it between two caps: merged in, it would head its list at the walk's address and the next cap would take it whole; Left, the caps held kept, `test_a_block_freed_into_t_between_caps`). A take no free block can hold is NoBlock without a carve (`largest()` first, exact: TLSF serves no request over it), so the firmware's failed-allocation hook prints no `[heap] FAILED` line for the arena a cut-up heap can't hold (`test_no_block_asks_no_carve`).
  - **Given back bottom-up**: a top block is freed, the block first and then its caps in carve order, only once every top block taken after it is given back. `commit()` gives the ranges' block back before the save lands; while the overflow's arena (carved after it, under it) is held, it waits, and the arena's give-back frees the arena, its caps, the ranges' block and its caps. So no free block ever opens above a held one.
  - **The overflow in one arena**: `statsfold::kArenaBytes`, 262,080 B (262,144 B carved, not 270,336 B), all 16 chunks' room, carved at the first chunk through the same hook (one block, one T, capped; no gaps), the chunks packed in it, given back whole when no chunk is in use (`Fold::held()` and `peak()` report its bytes). No room for it (no free block of 262 KB: the yardstick's scan after update 0 had none in 6 of 13 histories; mid-session, the spec's open question 5): NoBlock with no carve, and each chunk a carve of its own (`test_no_memory`, a chunk's carve failing in turn too), carved as fe61db9 carved them: `topAlloc()` gives the hold's caps back at the first chunk (`TopHold::uncap()`: T above the ranges' block open again), each chunk under spacers keeping 64 KB alone, nothing closed (`TopHold::take(..., close false)`: Plain); the first chunk is the hold's second entry (the ranges' block, given back by `commit()`, waits behind it), the rest past its two entries (Full) are freed at once when given back. So that hold is fe61db9's, PSRAM and layout (the review found the first build, which kept the ranges' caps and capped the first chunk, losing a full log fe61db9 folded: `test_the_arena_s_fallback_as_before`, the review's heap and 120 random ones, fe61db9's hooks saving a full log in 71: the firmware's loses none, the first build's 3).
  - **A decoder's room** (the recheck, 2026-11-18: hazard 5 for a track that follows one playing). That track opens during the hold, never held (main.cpp's `OutputHold` holds a start from a pause, a stop or a wait; a gap would be worse), its decoder's blocks carved while the hold's blocks and caps are held: GAPLESS.md 2.1's one decoder at a time gives the old one's blocks back first, but an MP3 gives back none (its decoder is the pinned block) and the session's first Opus carves 128,023 B (`OpusGenerator::allocBuffers()`: the page, the packet and a frame, kept for the session). The caps keep 64 KB and 1 KB free, not those blocks. The case: a remount whose re-read folds an overflow (a session behind), a track boundary inside its hold, the hold then the ranges' block with its T capped (about 71 KB mid-session: the spacers' margin counts the block) and the arena with its T' (about 78 KB), the largest free block what is left under them. In the model (Y5d: an MP3 playing, the session's first Opus opening after the re-read's first piece or its last, the session's blocks 0-224 KB more than the model's, the holes taken and not; 60 cases) 5e1d576's hold left the Opus no room in 36, 21 of them where fe61db9's hooks opened it, the first at 48 KB less free (the Opus after the first piece, where the arena holds every chunk's room at once and fe61db9 held one chunk). So a take asks room for the largest decoder's blocks when one may open before the block is given back (the trigger below; as first built, any take while a track played or a start waited) (`statsglue::kDecoderRoomBytes`, the Opus's 128,023 B; `TopHold::take(..., room, yield)`): with the block and every cap held, the largest free block must still take them (their carve, a thirty-second and 1 KB over: 133,047 B); short of that, every cap the hold holds is given back, the newest block's first (Room: T open again, as fe61db9 left it), and the arena, still short, is given back too (NoRoom: no block, so the fold carves its chunks one by one as before the caps, a piece at a time, as fe61db9 did). Now 16 of the 60 short, none where fe61db9's opened (fe61db9's own 19: its 16 chunks held too when the Opus opens after the last piece); main's layout at the model's own session in all four ways (the room there with every cap held: nothing given back) and in 26 of the 60; the arena given back in 40. A boot has the room (about 700 KB free): the boot's cases are unchanged. What it costs: where the room gives the caps or the arena back, that hold's layout is fe61db9's (Y5b, a FLAC playing, its holes taken: 498,588 B after the hold against main's 506,684 B, the Dc's join main's 491,508 B; a rule that can't know the next track keeps the Opus's room for a FLAC's too), and from 112 KB less free the re-read's chunks one by one find no room for all 16 (15 of the 60, each where fe61db9's didn't either, where 5e1d576's arena folded the log whole and left the Opus no room): the Opus first, the session already behind. The flag costs no RAM (1 B in `.bss`'s fill). **The trigger** (the final review, 2026-11-18). As first built every take asked while a track played or a start waited (`ListeningStats::pass()`), and the review found that wrong both ways. A start waiting at the arena's take can't open a decoder while the arena is held: `OutputHold` holds every start that isn't from Playing while overflow chunks are held, PlayGate's `statsHold` keeps it waiting, and the arena and its chunks are given back in one call (with the ranges' block waiting behind them). So the room asked for it could only give the caps back (Room) or the arena (NoRoom) for nothing. And while the ranges' block alone is held (no overflow chunk: a usual log, or the arena given back mid-scan by `fallBehind()`) `OutputHold` holds no start, so a Play from a stop or a pause after a take that asked nothing opened its decoder with T capped and the room never checked. Now the ranges' block asks at every take, whatever the player (it never yields: at worst its T open again, as fe61db9 left it), and the arena and a chunk only while a track plays (`s_trackPlaying`, the pass's `playing`; a chunk's take gives the caps back first, so its room changes nothing). The arena given back mid-scan leaves the ranges' block's caps as its take judged them, with the room. Nothing else asks again when the player changes in the hold: a start while the arena or a chunk is held waits, and a track playing at their take was asked for. In the model (U14): one free block of 480 KB, the ranges' block closed, then the arena with a start waiting: kept and capped (Closed), the largest free block 83,956 B (the start can't open yet), and once both are given back the Opus opens; 94cdf0c's rule gave the arena back (NoRoom). Through the glue a full log folds in the arena with the start waiting, one chunk at a time with a track playing, both saved. U15: one free block of 130-320 KB in 64 B steps, the player stopped, the ranges' request 1,284 or 3,076 B: the take gives its caps back (Room) in 1,024 heaps of each (138,560-204,032 B; 140,416-205,888 B), and the session's first Opus opens wherever fe61db9's hooks opened it; the take without the room (94cdf0c's for a stopped player) left it short in 960 and 962 of those heaps (138,560-199,936 B; 133,120-201,792 B), the packet's 61,440 B the one short in 928 and 930, the frame's 1,276 B in 32. Two of the 3,076 B heaps (133,120 and 133,184 B) leave the Opus short with the room asked too: the take leaves T open there (Margin), but its spacers keep the margin with the block carved (fix A), so the block sits 4 KB lower than under fe61db9's (at 61,444 B, not 65,540 B), and the packet finds no block; fix A's placement, not the trigger's (counted in U15, not hidden). The room is checked once, at the take: main's carves later in the hold that shrink the largest free block aren't seen (as before).
  - **The line** (one a take, owed: printed at the next pass, never inside the walk or the scan's frames): `[listen] the hold's top block (<which>): <bytes> B at 0x<p>; the <T> B free above it closed by <n> caps (<bytes> B) (<carved> B carved; <walks> walks in <us> us, the longest <us> us, <blocks> blocks)`, or why T was left open (in use above it: a probe or a FatFile is 4,224 B; no spacers; in a hole; the margin; a cap that came back elsewhere, both addresses; no PSRAM for a cap; the walk didn't meet it; the caps' most or the block above grown between caps; carved as before the caps, the arena's fallback). `<which>` by the take's size: `the ranges'`, `the overflow's arena` (262,080 B), `an overflow chunk (no room for the arena)` (16,384 B; the ranges' request is the fold's need + 4 B, need a multiple of 16, so never either). A take that walked nothing (no spacers, a chunk carved as before the caps) says `(not walked)`, not `0 B carved`. No free block for it: `NO PSRAM for its <bytes> B (no free block takes it; ...)` (asked of the largest free block first: no carve fails). The fallback's first chunk adds `the hold's <n> caps given back first`. Three take lines at most between passes (the ranges' block, the arena or the arena's refusal and the first chunk), none overwritten (a fourth is counted, `not told`); the fallback's chunks past the hold's two blocks one line once they stop coming: `[listen] the hold's top blocks: <n> more overflow chunks (no room for the arena) carved as before the caps past the hold's two blocks: not tracked, nothing closed, each freed when given back`. The boot's first take adds PSRAM's block count (`heap_caps_get_info()`'s `total_blocks`) and one `heap_caps_get_largest_free_block()` walk's time, the yardstick for the walks'. Since the recheck: a take is Closed only when the caps' bytes are T's plus 4 (the 12 B rest aside); another task's block at the rest's head between a cap and its walk is Left (`... the rest LEFT OPEN (..., or another task's block took the rest's head)`), not Closed. A Lost take (its walk didn't meet the block) says `(the block not met; 1 walk in <us> us, ...)`, not `0 B carved`. The decoder's room: `the <T> B free above it LEFT OPEN: with the hold's caps held no free block takes a decoder's 128023 B (<why>): the hold's <m> caps given back, its <n> among them` (Room; `<why>` `a start or a track playing` for the ranges' block, `a track playing` for the arena; a block that closed nothing itself: `with the hold's caps held ...: the hold's <m> caps given back, none of them its own`; with none held, what the take did stands), and for the arena `[listen] the hold's top block (the overflow's arena): its 262080 B GIVEN BACK at once: with it held no free block takes a decoder's 128023 B (a track playing), the hold's <m> caps given back first; its chunks one by one, as before the caps` (NoRoom; the first chunk's line follows, carved as before the caps).
  - **The walk's cost**: it runs under the PSRAM heap's lock (`multi_heap_walk` takes `xPortEnterCriticalTimeout`: this core's interrupts masked to level 3; a PSRAM heap call on the other core, Bluetooth's, the decode task's, the card worker's, spins with its own masked until it ends), as every `heap_caps_get_largest_free_block()` already does (two a spacer in `takeSpacers()`). A boot's take walks 3 times (walk, cap, walk, cap, walk), a full log's two takes up to 8. The callback compares and copies words on the caller's frame, nothing else; it gives up at 8,192 blocks before it meets the block (Lost). Flash code (`heap_caps_walk`, `multi_heap_walk`, `tlsf_walk_pool`); no IRAM.
- **Fix G: MARK's block by plain `psramAlloc`**, where any session block goes (at a top it would hold its cap for the session). In the model a 12-row and a 40-row card are main's line against a control that replays the read's carves (the work memory, the FatFile, the block kept), and their boot's join is bare main's (573,428 B both). The investigation's replay model, with the device-matching small holes, put a 40-row block (1,344 B) above the read's work memory and FatFile with a 15.7 KB hole under it for the session, two classes under bare main: the C++ model doesn't show it, and no card past 8 rows has run on the device. If it shows there, the read can carve the block again once its work memory is given back and move MARK's rows (`Fold::markBlock()`).
- **What it costs**: PSRAM during the hold only (0.6-4 s at a boot): in the model's boot heap the ranges' block's caps take T's 63,468 B (2 caps) and, with a full log, the arena's T 73,720 B more (3 caps): about 400 KB held at a full log's peak against about 272 KB before (free about 439 KB meanwhile; before, more free but its largest split by the chunks' gaps). Any overflow, one chunk or 16, carves the whole arena and its caps: about 400 KB held for the hold where a one-chunk overflow (41-859 records: a 0.14.0 card's first stats boot, a session whose saves failed) held about 18 KB before (one 16,900 B chunk and the 1,316 B ranges' block); the fold's `[listen]` line (`the overflow N B at the peak`) and `H`'s RAM line report the arena's 262,080 B. Owner's call: an arena sized to what the scan can still need. No room for the arena: the chunks as before the caps, the hold's caps given back (fe61db9's PSRAM for that hold). A decoder's room (above, the recheck; since the final review the ranges' block's at every take, the arena's and a chunk's while a track plays): the caps given back, and the arena, when the largest free block wouldn't take the Opus's buffers with them held (fe61db9's PSRAM and layout for that hold; at a boot the room is there and nothing is given back, at the model's mid-session remount with an MP3 playing too, but with a FLAC playing and the holes taken the arena is given back: Y5b). A start waits while the overflow is held, Auto DJ's join and an asked library update while the scan's blocks are (unchanged), and the first frame (19.6 KB), the scan's and the save's FatFiles and work memory, a queue and the card worker's walk fit, and so does a track that follows one playing, its decoder opening in the hold, wherever fe61db9's hold left it room (Y5d: an MP3's next track the session's first Opus; before the recheck short in 21 of 60 such cases where fe61db9's opened it). Internal RAM (since the review's fixes, 2026-11-18): `.dram0.bss` 36,296 B, 248 B over the move's 36,048 B and 2,344 B over S0's (the owed lines `s_tl` 176 B: three lines, the first take's figures and the counts; the hold `s_top` 52 B, and 4 B of fill that the room's flag takes 1 B of (`s_decoderMayOpen` since the recheck, `s_trackPlaying` since the final review); the object 2,008 B: 16 B more, `Memory::topRelease` and the Fold's arena pointer with their padding; `.dram0.data` unchanged); RAM 61,160 B to 61,408 B (`_heap_start` 248 B higher: the DRAM heap 79,040 B). Against G5 (the headphones' run: about 6 KB over its floors) that is 248 B of it. (As first built, 8cd3708: 36,240 B, the lines 120 B; RAM 61,352 B, the DRAM heap 79,096 B.) Flash 4,470,575 B on core2 as first built (3,440 B over c3f1f09's 4,467,135 B; the walk's framework functions, `heap_caps_walk`, `heap_caps_walker`, `multi_heap_walk` and `tlsf_walk_pool`, in flash text), 4,471,547 B since the review (972 B more: `WalkAbove`, `uncap()`, the lines; `WalkAbove::step` flash text too; core2-dio 4,471,579 B), 4,472,567 B since the recheck (1,020 B more: the room, the lines; core2-dio 4,472,599 B; RAM unchanged), 4,472,587 B since the final review (20 B more: the trigger, the lines' reason; core2-dio 4,472,619 B; RAM unchanged); IRAM left 5,157 B, S0's 25 `libc_a-*` objects in `.iram0.text`: unchanged.
