### 9.9 S5: the owner's card

S5a after S1 is merged and before any release or main build carrying S1 boots the music card; S5b (step 4) after S2. Only with the owner's go-ahead.

Before step 1: the board's settings and image backed up (9.1). After each boot, `z`. No taps, no `n`, `p` or space.

1. Copies of the card's `/.player/plays*.jnl`, read-only from a PC reader: `python tools/playlog.py read` gives the plays, deviceIds and epochs the first boot will fold.
2. The stats firmware boots with the card. **Nothing is played.** `H`, `Hm`: the fold equals the copies' count by 4.1's rules (`statsref`); the save; the trim of ACKed files only.
3. The owner syncs on the PC (the terminal writes ACKS); the next boot: no fold, the trim, `H` unchanged.
4. (S5b) `Hr`, `Hw`, `Hl0`: the lists on the real library (19.5k tracks); the walk's time; orphans.

After step 4: the board is left on the stats build, with the owner's word. A 0.14.0 image with this card undercounts (5.12).

**Effort:** 0.5-1 (guess; S5a 0.25-0.5, S5b 0.25-0.5), plus the owner's sync.

### 9.10 S6: the time zone

Free-standing from S3 on; two repositories.

- **C26, firmware:**
  - `HostLink::timeLine` reads an optional field 1 (−720 to +840; anything else: a range error) into `HostLink::Out`;
  - NVS `clock/tz`, an i16, schema 7, `migrate(6, 7)` doing nothing;
  - `tzOffsetMin` from it into `statsview`/`statstext`; Week's sub drops "(UTC)".
  - Tests: `test_host_link`, `test_nvs_layout`, `test_ui_stats`.
  - Docs: `docs/HOST-STATUS.md`'s `@time` (`:304-320`, "Always UTC").
- **C27, terminal:** `src/device/desk.rs` sends `@time <unix-ms> <minutes>` from `tz::local()` when it gives a zone: on terminal main after #56 lands, or on #56 once main (with 5eac90c) is merged into it. At 3edd4a4, `tz::local()` gives no zone on Windows (terminal `src/admin/tz.rs:6-7` there), and the owner's PC runs Windows. Firmware before S6 ignores the field.

**Done when:**
```
pio test -e native -f test_host_link -f test_nvs_layout -f test_ui_stats
pio test -e native
pio run -e core2 -e core2-dio
```
and the terminal's `cargo test` for `desk.rs`.

**Device** (the spare, silent mode): `@time <ms> 60`, then `@status`; Week's sub without "(UTC)"; a reset keeps `clock/tz`; `@time <ms>` with no field leaves it; `[nvs] schema 6 -> 7: ...` (`src/app/NvsSchema.cpp:85`) on the backed-up settings, then the settings put back (9.1).

**Effort:** 1.5-2.5 (guess, both repositories).

**Total:** about 19-28.5 agent-days, a guess (S0 1-1.5, S1 5-7.5, S2 4-6, S3 6-8.5, S4 1-1.5, S5a and S5b 0.5-1, S6 1.5-2.5). S1 grew with the review's cases (a partial scan, the base's faults, the card's copy changed, the end's rotation, the twin, firstAt, retention) and runs G (a throwaway build) and L; S2 with the scheduler's lanes and the join's gate. The mood plan said 15-24, and parallel sessions built its S0-S5 in about a calendar day.

A slow pass is a UI hitch, not an audio risk: the sinks draw on a ring of about 1.5 s (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:83`).

**S0 baselines** (MEASURED-HOST, 2026-11-11, on this Windows box). The tree was cad1449: main 241642d's code with C0-C2's docs. Main since bc29b54 changed only `tools/version.py` (#58, NEXT_RELEASE 0.15.0) and docs, so this is bc29b54's firmware with the next version's string. Every later stage compares against these.
- **Host tests:** `pio test -e native` from Git Bash (PlatformIO Core 6.2.0): 108 suites, 2,286 test cases, 2,286 succeeded, 0 failed, in 18 min 46 s.
- **`pio run -e core2`** (PowerShell, MSYSTEM unset; a clean build, 8 min 19 s): flash 4,423,691 B (70.3 % of 6,291,456; `flash_guard: ok: app 4.31 MB = 72% of the 6.00 MB slot`), 12 B over 5ea8d34's 4,423,679 B (the version string). RAM 59,064 B (PlatformIO's figure: `.dram0.data` 25,112 B and `.dram0.bss` 33,952 B; 65,512 B of `dram0_0_seg` left for the heap). IRAM 5,161 B left: `iram0_0_seg` ends at 0x400A0000 and `.iram0.text` at 0x4009EBD7. `iram_diet: 51 of 51 libc objects moved to flash`.
- **The link map's `libc_a-*` objects in `.iram0.text`** (`.pio/build/core2/firmware.map`, the `libc_a-<name>.o` inputs between `.iram0.text` and `.dram0.data` in its memory map), 25: bzero, div, envlock, getenv_r, isspace, memchr, memcmp, memcpy, memmove, memset, strcat, strchr, strcmp, strcpy, strcspn, strlcat, strlcpy, strlen, strncmp, strncpy, strnlen, strrchr, strspn, strstr, tolower. A stage that adds a name to this list has linked a pinned object; IRAM left must stay 5,161 B.

## 13. Risks

What would change the plan:

- **The save is felt.** If S1 I or H shows the boot's save over 0.3 s, or a compaction over 1.5 s, with an MP3, or any break: move it to a worker job after Ready, and trim at a boot only what the card's MARK already covers. Each boot's slots are then trimmed one boot later, and folds during the worker's save need a frozen snapshot (its memory measured first).
- **The format is primary data.** A mistake found after S1's first release stays on cards: every later firmware reads v1. S0's reference and S1's proof are the guard. If S1 ships alone, that release is the first real-card run, so S5's steps 1-2 are S5a, run before that release.
- **A downgrade, or a 0.14.0 unit** with the card, trims without folding: an undercount, counted in HEAD's gap and said in the release notes. No double count.
- **The rotation hole.** A rotation deletes (or moves to a twin) a `plays.1.jnl` holding plays the card's MARK doesn't cover, and no save lands before the restart: those plays are lost to the stats (5.5). As built since the rework (5.14), S1 as first built's two give-back causes are closed, and others are open where S1 as first built kept the plays in RAM and a later save could still land (5.14's list). The sharpest needs one play, not a rotation's 6,553 (the review): a full log (13,106 slots) whose boot save fails all four of its tries over the boot's waits (about 1.4 s), by a fault that clears before the first play ends. A full log's `plays.jnl` is at its cap, so that play rotates, its save refused (behind), and up to 6,553 plays of `plays.1.jnl` are lost to the stats (`boot-overflow-full-rotation`). The others: a log not full whose boot save didn't land with an overflow, a session whose records' saves fail or are refused to 96, a held tmp lost before its promotion, a section damaged under the session; each then needs `plays.jnl`'s room in plays before a rotation. A session whose saves fail before it is behind loses the plays of every `plays.1.jnl` a rotation took out meanwhile too, held only in the records behind drops (`records-fail-rotations`: 77 at the fixtures' cap of 16; the rework review). Holding the full log's overflow until that first rotation would close the sharpest (the join waiting up to a track, and steps failing in the model); folding the log again above the card's MARK, oldest file first, in windows of 96 records with a save each, would close the hole for good (and make behind unneeded): about 100 saves for a full log and a second reader of the log. Both the owner's call; the owner's default is the hole documented, not closed. As built, it happens when:
  - the rotation's save fails (a full or failing card), or its tmp is held and not promoted before the restart (5.5, step 5);
  - the stats are Off this session (Unread, Newer, no memory for the fold, the folds dropped while ending);
  - this session's saves are refused (a partial scan, a changed `stats.bin`, behind its log: 5.5);
  - the rotation comes in the orderly end's flush with under 1 s of its budget left (`src/app/PlayHistory.cpp:307`; `lib/core/PlayLog.cpp:505, 437`);
  - a full log's boot save fails all four of its tries, by a fault that clears before the first play ends: that play rotates, its save refused (behind), and up to 6,553 plays of `plays.1.jnl` are lost (`boot-overflow-full-rotation`: 128 at the fixtures' cap of 128; its `-retry` twin, the second try landing, 0);
  - the session is behind its log otherwise (the records full after a save that didn't land or was refused, a boot overflow not saved in a log that isn't full, a held tmp lost before its promotion, a section damaged), and a rotation follows (`records-behind-rotation`: 64 at the cap of 64; `section-damaged` loses its 4 to the next boot's Corrupt read instead);
  - the session's saves fail or are refused until it is behind, and rotations came before: each took out a `plays.1.jnl` whose plays were in the records alone, which behind drops (`records-fail-rotations`: 77 at the cap of 16; the rework review).
  Each but the full log's needs a rotation after the save that didn't land or the refusal: `plays.jnl`'s room in plays, up to 6,553 (as S1 as first built's needed the second rotation of a session, or a boot whose save failed or was skipped). statsref's sessions assert each count exactly (R2), and `test_stats_store` runs them through the firmware's glue (R5). S1 as first built's two give-back causes (a rotation while the blocks were given back, its own save unlanded: `update-rotation-unsaved`; one after a take-back that left RAM behind: `update-behind-rotation`, 2 lost) closed with the give-back.
- **The resident set's budget** (5.14). In the model a set held from setup costs by its bytes, wherever it sits. Up to about 4 KB it fails only steps main's own variation fails too, and from 8 KB steps the control doesn't (8,192 B: 3 of 360 with the device's decoder requests, 21 with the old shapes; 12 KB: 11 and 31). The edges in between move with the decoders' shapes (4,096 B: 0 or 4; 1,024 B: 1 or 0), so 3,584 B is a budget held by the `static_assert` and confirmed by the device re-run, not an edge the model measured. Nothing joins the block later unless it comes out of it, or R1's case still passes and the device confirms it. S2's plan (6.1, 6.5, 11) holds a hash → id map (16.9-67.6 KB) and the album bits resident: as planned they can't stay. S2 must keep them for a rank's or a page's life, carved at the top and given back, or redesign them before it starts; S3's week (96 B) fits in the state, S6's hours (1,536 B) don't.
- **The model is noise-bound, and doesn't reproduce round 2.** Main's own variation (its queue block 2,048 B longer) fails 2 of the wide set's 360 steps with the device's decoder requests and 6 with the old shapes, and main's joined moves with the history alone (H4's from 106,484 to 294,900 B in the device's shape). On round 2's own sequence (5.14's history D) the model gives S1 minus main -69,632, 0 and +36,864 B where the device showed 0, -106,496 and -67,584 B; the decoders' shapes alone move a step by 110 KB. The device has one sample per condition, and its room is about 100 KB under the model's. So the device re-run against main's figures (5.14's build plan, within the class of main's own figure) decides, not the model.
- **The records' save is felt.** It comes once in 64 plays, 0.08-0.2 s typical and 0.35-0.7 s at the ceiling on the loop, at a pass the write gate lets through, and at 96 records even in an update's fence, while the build runs on the worker (a carve and a free in one call, as the rotation's save already may). If run H or M shows a `[ui] slow pass` from it, or an underrun: cut it into pieces across passes (the records it holds are fixed at its start; nothing else writes `stats.bin` meanwhile), or move it to the worker as the first risk says. Its stack (R5's sums): 1,488 B from `PlayHistory::loop`, 2,080 B from `loop()`'s own, under the rotation's save (11).
- **A decoder opening under the boot's held blocks.** A tap on Play during the boot's scan opens a decoder while the stats' scan blocks are held. With the device's decoder requests (5.14): the ranges' 3,072 B cost nothing wherever they sit; the 10,896 B work memory the design first held cost up to 8 steps of 360, so it is no longer held; a full log's overflow (about 270 KB, about 1.4 s and its save, once a card) fails 0-29 steps by order and placement, none when the Opus opens after it. So a track's start the listener asks waits while overflow chunks are held (once a card, about 1.5-3 s late); run I measures the overflow on the device. If the owner would rather not hold a start, the cost is the model's 0-29 steps for that session. As built (R6): a start waits during every boot's scan (its first piece reserves one chunk), and with the firmware's blocks the model finds the Opus under the scan's blocks fails no step in either shape; with the old shapes it costs the boot's join 16,384 B more than the order's own cost. As built (the rework review): a start waits only while a full log's fold holds chunks (the scan reserves no look-ahead), once a card; and the wait is load-bearing after all: with the ranges' block at its largest (ACKS's 16 rows above MARK, 3,076 B) the Opus under a full log's overflow fails 10 of 65 steps and the boot's join 65,536 B under main's with the Opus first (5.14's R6 notes); with 1,284 B none, wherever the start comes in the scan.
- **The scan and a library update wait for each other** (the review). The boot's scan doesn't begin while an update is asked, and an update asked once the scan has begun waits in Asked for its blocks: up to about 1.4 s and the save for a full log, and the boot's waits (about 1.4 s) more when that save fails. Without these the model fails 15-48 steps of 360 (the scan's blocks held through, or carved during, update 0's build); R1's case, with the design's own scan blocks (a full log's ranges and 16 chunks), 24-48; R6's, with the firmware's, 25-62. A post-sync rebuild at the boot (round 2's boot 2) is the case: the scan then waits for the build, the plays in the Tracker.
- **A section of `stats.bin` damaged under the session** (the review). Every save now reads the card's sections, so a CRC that fails with the header unchanged stops the session's saves at once (behind, `H` naming it), and the next boot reads the file Corrupt and renames it `stats.bad` (5.4): HEAD's totals, HOUR, DAYS, RCNT and TRKD are lost, and the stats start again from what the log still holds. S1 as built healed it at the next save from RAM's copy. It needs the card's data to change between reads without a write of ours (a failing sector); the console says why.
- **The boot's hold splitting PSRAM** (B1, 2026-11-17; 5.14, "The hold's T"): the free block the hold's top blocks left above them, and a full log's gaps between chunks, took a long-lived carve made during the hold (the first frame's canvas, a queue) and split the largest free block for the session (three classes at the join on I-3m, twelve on I-1; free unchanged). Fixed by A + G on the host; what stays: a cap that misses (another task's carve first), another task's block at T's rest's head between a cap and its walk, no spacers, a block in a hole, a block above that grows between caps (another task's free), a decoder's room while a track plays (the caps given back, and the arena) or no room for the arena (its chunks then carved as fe61db9 carved them, the hold's caps given back) leave that hold as before, and its `[listen]` line says so (the rest's head said Closed until the recheck); the device re-run owed.
- **PSRAM at the ceiling** (S1 as first built, history, and S2's plan; the rework held 3,200 B, at most 3,584 B, whatever P, until the move to internal RAM: 0 resident since). A card where nearly every track has been played takes about 267 KB resident (in chunks) and up to about 195 KB more during a walk. A failed allocation leaves names as "Counting plays…" and never loses data. Fallbacks: 6.7's ladder; u32 prefixes for the played set (4 B, confirmed by the full hash on a match), album bits from fewer chunks. Held through the updates, S1's blocks alone failed the model's pass rule at P = 19,478's resident ceiling (194,880 B) and after a full never-synced log's fold, whether its save landed or not (S1 C8). The owner chose to give them back in updates (2026-11-11), and the model passes the mood plan's rule for every shape, and since S1's gate after the give-back the device's 16,368 B of room over the line after a FLAC update too: with 16,384 B chunks the re-read at P = 19,478 cost joined one chunk, 16,384 B (the give-back review), a chunk over 11's budget that chunks of whole rows took out (6.7). S1 L on the device measures it.
- **The give-back's cost** (S1 as first built, history: the rework removed the give-back, 5.14). Each library update saves `stats.bin` first when the fold holds new plays, in the fence's pass (the update's safe point, and since the give-back review a pass the write gate lets through: the step waits in Asked for one), and reads it back whole, its base too (467 KB at 19,478 played), once the update and the rejoin are over (a read the card fails made again, the boot's waits apart). If S1 L shows either felt (a `[ui] slow pass`, an underrun): the re-read can skip the base's whole read when `stats.bin` is the copy the session stood on. A session whose give-back's save didn't land shows fewer plays until the next boot, saves nothing more, and loses to a later rotation that session the plays in `plays.1.jnl` RAM gave up (5.5). If that hole matters, the code's answer is to fold the log again above the card's MARK at the take-back (the remount's path over `plays.1.jnl` and `plays.jnl`), which makes the behind state unneeded: the owner's call.
- **Orphans.** Renames, Opus conversions and NFC/NFD changes take plays out of the shelves until ORIG is followed. The totals stay right.
- **RCNT's cap** shortens the 30-day window for very heavy listening. Its cell says how many days.
- **UTC days** mark the wrong "today" in the listener's evening west of UTC (or morning east of it) until S6. O4 accepted that.
- **Translations** are machine-made; 684 texts owe a native review (666 new, 18 short forms of ARB words).
- **The tray's splits** are untested on this panel.
- **The scratch spec and frames** go with Windows' temp cleanup in about a week. This plan inlines the spec; S0's C1 copies the frames from the canvas, which persists.
- **A `stats.bin` the card fails to read at every boot** (a bad sector) keeps the stats off and the trims held. The log stays bounded by the rotation, which then deletes plays no MARK covers (the rotation hole); the console says why; a disk check is the fix.
- **Two hashings with Auto DJ on.** The library is hashed twice after a Live (the join's and the stats' walk): about 0.1 s more CPU. If S2 F shows it felt, the observer ride (14) comes back.

## 14. Considered and left out

**Other directions on the canvas.**
- A, Glance: a stats screen of tiles, a 4 x 64 px period tray at the right edge, a month view. Its data model, the entry row's bars, the empty state and the stat value were grafted into C.
- B, Recap: a story of pages (the week, the hours, the top, the find), reached from the Dance tab. Its big day, its "found by", its Auto DJ rate and the undated anchor to the sync were grafted. Its overlay band and its hash-to-track lookup were not.
- A dedicated tab: the tab bar's four tabs stay; the stats live where the listener browses.

**Taken out by the owner's decisions.**
- The plays line on album track rows (frame AlbumPlays; O3), and `stats.not_yet*`.
- A filter by deviceId (O2), and TRKS's "another device's" flag bit.
- Not folding `z`'s plays (O6).

**Other shapes for the data.**
- Three files renamed one by one: disagree after a cut (1.1).
- TRKS rewritten whole at every boot: up to 467 KB a boot at the ceiling.
- A log-only v1, counting the log at each boot: every trim and rotation loses history.
- Counters in NVS: a write a play, against `src/app/NvsLater.h:19-21`.
- DAYS alone, without HOUR: no local days when the zone arrives.
- HOURS for 13 months (the spec): nothing in v1 shows hours past a week; DAYS keeps the shape of a year at 6.4 KB.
- Folding drop notes into a lost figure: they carry no seq, MARK can't dedupe them, and on a card never synced each note counts plays already folded.
- A save mid-session on a timer: each costs a 0.05-0.4 s loop hold, and gains nothing the next boot's fold doesn't. The rotation's save and `Hs` are the only ones, both synchronous, so no fold happens under them.
- A staged boot fold beside the base: reading `stats.bin` again at a restarted scan does the same with no second copy.
- A resident TRKS: 53 KB typical but 467 KB at the ceiling.
- The worker saving before `finishScan()`: no window (1.1).

**Other shapes for the map.**
- The spec's id-keyed played-track table (8 B a played track, dense past half the library): can't name the rankers' hashes, and is dropped at every fence.
- A dense 4 B-a-track array: Auto DJ's filters block's size class, which once failed to place.
- Riding Auto DJ's join (an observer in `hashSlice`, `lib/core/AutoDjJoin.cpp:631-643`): saves about 0.1 s of CPU after a Live with Auto DJ on, which the owner keeps off (`docs/MOOD-FILTERS.md:1525`); at the boot the join starts before the stats' lists exist. The code is shared; the pass is not.
- `Join::setPathHash()` as the hook: it turns the folder states off (6.2).
- A walk on the card worker: the index isn't thread-safe (`lib/core/LibraryIndex.h:100`), and the fence clears it on the loop.

**Other shapes for the UI.**
- The artist page's " · N plays" sums: left out by this plan's reading of O3 (3.2), not by the owner.
- A "Today" window and a 4-cell tray: under the panel's right-edge skew.
- "Per day" filler rows, a "New to you" row with no list: dropped by the design's judges.
- An hour-of-day view: needs the zone, and a room the design didn't have.
- Swipes between windows: the house has no swipe on lists.
- Inert segments drawn dim: dropped; offered cells only.
- An inert NP row for a first play: no row instead.

**Later, not v1.**
- mStream's per-track counts on the card (spec 8.6): Not played yet means never, counts include other clients.
- An artist page "Top tracks" row.
- Following renames through ORIG's audioHash.
- Dating undated plays back from a later slot of the same boot (2.20.6 item 7), in the live and the boot's folds alike.
